Unpinned and inspector cleared in `mpLeave`, `leaveHotseat`, `mpAbandonReconnect` and a `joined` into another room. > **17 October 2026, night (THE REVIEW'S THREE LOCKS — client v2.6.163 / server v2.6.47 / bot v0.6.206 beside the suite; purge the client AND restart the server):** **Client:** `escapeHtml(s)` = `& < > " '` → `&amp; &lt; &gt; &quot; &#39;` (null → ''); `esc(s)` wraps it. Every attribute built from a peer's string goes through it (clan symbols' `alt`/`title` in `updateL4GlobeHTML`, `updateTurnText`, `renderRemote`, `buildMat`'s pool globe, `renderL3`, `buildL2mat`, `sayBubble`; names in `statRow`/`renderStats` `title`/`data-name`, the lobby's `data-name`; reveal links' `data-name`). Sinks that are TEXT must get raw strings: `toast()` (textContent), `uiDialog` messages, `.title=` properties (`cardTitle`), menu labels (`showMenu` items; `renderItems` sets textContent -- the log link's `⋖ Target:` row was escaped until the review). A static test refuses `toast(...esc(` and a `label` built with esc. The error ring (`errRing`) decodes entities after stripping tags. The bots' `stripTags` (v0.6.178+) and the rig's `strip` decode `&quot;`/`&#39;`; chat payloads were never escaped. **Server:** `cleanName` strips `[\u0000-\u001f\u007f<>"]` (names, rooms, watchers, `onto.who`, `toName`, owner/controller); `cleanCard` strips `[\u0000-\u001f\u007f<>=]` (card names keep quotes); `cleanSym(s)=cleanCard(s)` minus `"'` (pub `clan`, card `path`); `cleanId` for pub card `id`/`host`/`attached[]`/`target.cid`, the duel `cid`, `give.attachTo`. `wsAttach(sock, cb)`: `cb.msg(text)` / `cb.ctrl()` (ping or pong) may return false → the reading ends (`dead`); `fail()` = dead + close frame + destroy after 400 ms; checks per frame: RSV bits, ctrl op > 0xA / !fin / len7 > `MAX_CTRL` (125), data op > 0x1 (binary refused), a continuation without `fragOp`, a new message with `fragOp` set, `frags.length > MAX_FRAGS` (64), `fragLen > MAX_MSG`; unmasked → accepted, `[ws] an unmasked client frame` logged once per connection. `connRateOk(conn)` = the 80/10 s bucket (updates `conn.last`; over → `err` 'Slow down — too many messages.' + `wsClose`, false); the upgrade handler passes `msg: raw => { if(!connRateOk(conn)) return false; handle(conn, raw); }` and `ctrl: () => connRateOk(conn)`. `adminLocked(ip)` = `strikes(ipLogAuth, ip, ERR_WIN) >= (isLoopback(ip) ? ERR_MAX*4 : ERR_MAX)`, read before `passMatch` by `hello{admin}` (→ `err` 'Too many wrong admin passwords. Wait a bit.'), `handleLogFetch` and `handleMaintenance` (→ 404); a wrong key `strike(ipLogAuth, ip)`. `roomUnsnap` cleans the room and player names (cleanName) and `restore()` keys rooms by the cleaned name; `saveUnsnap(s)` (called by `loadSaves`) cleans saved players' names and re-runs `sanitizePub` on `pub` (false) and `pubOwn` (true). `fxCard` cleans names with `cleanCard`, as do the conceal-flip name and the `verb`. Unmasked frames → `wsUnmaskedNote()`: a count, logged at most once a minute. **Harness:** `HTML_REC` (module level) records `innerHTML`/`outerHTML` sets and `insertAdjacentHTML(pos, html)` while `api.__htmlRec.start()` is on; exposes `escapeHtml`, `esc`, `renderL3`, `renderL2`, `renderL4`, `renderRemote`, `renderStats`, `renderLobby`, `updateTurnText`, `sayBubble`, `previewRemote`, `get toast`; `loadServer` exposes `wsAttach`, `connRateOk`, `MAX_FRAGS`, `MAX_CTRL`, `RATE_N`, `cleanName`, `cleanCard`, `cleanSym`, `sanitizePub`, `ERR_WIN`. Bot v0.6.205–206 consumed (guide re-embedded). Review items B and C stay open (journal 17 Oct).

> **16 October 2026, evening (THE VERDICT WAIT — client v2.6.162 / server v2.6.46 / bot v0.6.204 beside the suite; pure client, purge):** Consumes bot v0.6.202–204 (handoffs of 16 Oct; reply `handoffs/REPLY-to-bot-2026-10-16-v0.6.204.md`). **`BOT_STEP_LABEL['resolve-verdict']='Declare verdict'`** -- bot v0.6.204's rung: its block on a human's action waits for the actor (`_vWait`; status `asking`, `block verdict (NAME)`, `until` null in Turn-based and while its question is still queued); `step()` logs `NAME: block verdict declared from the table controls.` and declares from the counter (`_vwResolve('button')`). `botGiveWordTargets` is a filter (v2.6.140), so the Turn-based ▶ already stepped it; its title and the two comments name the block verdict. Help ▸ Bots re-embedded (`embed-help.js bots`; their nav chip says v0.6.203, the footer v0.6.204 -- theirs). **Tests:** the v2.6.140 LIVE test's `waitOnHuman(ms, armed)` (their §3 fix: v0.6.204 arms a wait when its question is ON THE WIRE, so the first steppable status can have `until: null`); a sync test of the label, the row, the dash with a live button and the ▶ title; two LIVE tests with the real bot through `hotBridge` -- both stub `b.decide('block-as-target')` to block and give the bot a ready vampire with an empty hand: Turn-based (the question, the row, `botGiveWord()`, the §12 line with `who` = the bot, `avr.mode` block → combat) and Standard 3 s (inside `MP_HANDLERS.chat` for the question `status().until` is still null; right after, the deadline is 2.7–3.3 s from the question; the bot's verdict on the clock). The verdict line is read from the `who` the bridge hands `MP_HANDLERS.log`, which is also what labels the bubble (`maybeVerdictPulse(html, m.who, seat)`). **What the bot's verdict does to the counter:** it writes no `tally` null (a human's `avrResolve` does): when the block holds, its `_resolveCombat` writes `mode:'combat'` (block → combat directly, as after a bot actor's own verdict); when it fails, the counter stays in block mode for the rest of the action and the actor's `resolvePlayedActions` (It resolves) or `actStart` (the next action) closes it. A second human Resolve on it writes a second verdict line; the bot reopens its window (Hold on) and the bleed still lands once. **Rig v1.1.0 / report v1.1.0:** `QUESTION` += `add stealth|may block it`; `samplePanel` records `askArmed` (the first sample whose `left` is a number while an ask is open, or at the ask's creation when the clock already runs); the report's `clock` column = `armed - lag` (≈ 0..0.35 s with v0.6.204, negative = the clock ran before the question) and `clock before the question: N` per kind; the 6 s status fallback sets `a.waitClock` instead of firing when the ask has neither a clock (`armT`) nor a question (`chatT`), and the `askArmed` branch then arms the reflex `delayMs` later; `questionFits(a, msg)` = `QUESTION` plus, for a `combat step NAME (…)` ask, that step's own question (`STEP_Q`, the bot's `Q[]` in `_phaseStep`, `damage` = `Damage: prevent`). **PROTOCOL §12:** the three bot-emitted `NAME combat — VAMPIRE does not maneuver. / has nothing before strikes. / does not press.` lines, the combat-ends shape note, who sends the Block verdict line; §12.0: the bots' log-tier moves and their two new questions (not frozen). **Measured in headless Chromium (paceMs 3100):** the bot's `_drain` waits `max(200, chatPaceMs ?? round(paceMs × 1.6))` = 4.96 s after any chat line, so everything after its question waits: the verdict line 4.96 s after the question (the clock decided at 3.04 s), 4.89 s after a Declare verdict click (decided in 0.08 s), the booking of a `+1` typed 0.06 s after the question 4.96 s after the question (the beat counts from the bot's last chat line, so a later `+1` waits less). Reported to the bot project.

> **16 October 2026, late afternoon (THE VISIT FROM ABOVE — client v2.6.161 / server v2.6.46 / bot v0.6.201 beside the suite; pure client, purge):** **`switchView`:** after `net.view=target` and the body-class toggles, `if(prev==='ov' && typeof target==='number') enterL2();` -- a seat visit from the helicopter view stands on L2, as a visit from L2 does (that path never exits L2). Before, `exitL3()` removed `l2mode`/`l3mode`/`l2cols` and the visit stood on the retired L1 stage: without `l2cols`, `fitBoard()` scales the fixed 1004 × 616 board (`min(ta.w/1004, ta.h/616)`), and `#l2dock` (shown only under `#board.l2mode` or `.l3mode`) was gone. `enterL2()` runs before `renderReady()`/`renderHand()`, so they see the board a visit from L2 sees; `body.inL2` stays set when such a visit then goes to L3, which is harmless (`body.inov .l2peektab` hides the peek tabs) and already happened on the L2 → visit → L3 path. Classic (L4 → a number) still exits to the L1 stage by design. **`setAside` settle:** module-level `_asideTe` (the newest call's `transitionend` listener) and `_asideBack` (its 250 ms backstop); a new call removes the older listener and clears the older backstop. The listener counts only the panel's OWN width (`e.target===_asideEl`, a child's transition bubbles there too) and always sends the final resize; the backstop fires only if that has not happened. Before, `_asideSettled` let whichever came first win, so a backstop that fired before a late transition swallowed its `transitionend`, and everything measured mid-transition stayed -- seen in headless Chromium: no frame for 225 ms after the view switch while timers ran, the transition started at 215 ms and ended at 365 ms, the backstop at 250 ms measured the panel at 24 px. Harness: `setAside`, `switchView` and `__win` (the window stub) exposed.

> **13 October 2026, evening (THE FACE DECIDES — client v2.6.149 / server v2.6.45 / bot v0.6.198 beside the suite; purge the client AND restart the server):** **Zone on the free board** — `freeFaceZone(c, zone)`: on `l4on()`, for a crypt card without `host`, `ready` + face down → `uncontrolled`, `uncontrolled` + face up → `ready`; torpor and library cards are never touched. `move()` computes the face from the ASKED zone (unchanged rules), then re-homes by `freeFaceZone`; coordinates follow the asked zone: `asked==='ready'` or `opts.disp` → display coords through `readyToCanon`, else `opts.x/y` raw (canonical). On the free board an `uncontrolled` card's `z` is bumped like ready/torpor. `freeFaceSync(c)` re-homes after a flip without moving the card (both zones are canonical felt coords there) and bumps `z`; called by `tap` (double-click), the card menu's Flip, the `ctrl` flip, `groupFlipUp`, `flipSelected`. `freeFaceSyncAll()` runs in `enterL4`. `freeDragKeepsZone(c, from)`: on the free board a crypt card dragged from torpor, or face down from uncontrolled, never attaches (`attachTargetAt` is skipped). The drops ask `dz = (l4on() && from==='torpor') ? 'torpor' : 'ready'` with `disp:true`; the drop's log line names where the card ENDED (none for a reposition). Minion counts (`unlockedMinions`, `renderStats` own + pubs, the L3 mat label, `buildMat`, the seat-visit header) skip face-down cards. `restoreFromPub` and `given` pass `faceDown` into `move()`. `parkWhere(kind)` = 'on the table' for a crypt card on the free board, else 'in the ready region'. **Torpor on the free board** — Leave torpor = `move(c,'ready')` with no coordinates (stays where it lies); Structured keeps `zoneRect('ready')` + 24. A torpored card carries `.torp` (own: one pass in `layout()`; others: `addRCard` when `ctx && zone==='torpor' && l4on()`); CSS `#board.l4mode .card.torp::after{content:'TORPOR'}` + dimmed art. **Turn count** — `advanceTurnCounter` ends with `updateL3Timer()`; the match-clock tick calls it too. `joined` zeroes `net.turnNo`/`roundNo` unless it is an automatic reconnect (`was==='re'`) into the SAME room (`wasRoom===m.room`); `net.tnSync = !m.lobby` and `tnAdopt(m.myPub)`; `took` (a seat taken over, a loaded match) opens `net.tnSync` and adopts from `m.pub` (a loaded match first zeroes the count); `board` calls `tnAdopt(m.pub)` while `net.tnSync`; `turn` sets `net.tnSync=false`. `tnAdopt` takes `pub.tn`/`pub.rn` only when BOTH are positive and `tn` is higher than the client's count (caps 9999), and refreshes the label and the phase counts. The match clock writes the felt in its tick, at its end and when it is cleared. `buildPub` carries `tn`/`rn` when `inRoom()` and `turnNo>0`. srv-2.6.45 `sanitizePub` keeps `tn`/`rn` (positive ints ≤ 9999) in `pub` and `pubOwn`. **Played tray** — `playedPinned`, `playedShowT`, `playedHideT`, `playedAutoPin` are declared beside `avr` (`avrRender` reads them at start-up): `pinPlayedAuto()` pins only a closed tray and sets it; `pinPlayed()` / `closePlayed()` clear it; `avrRender()` with no mode calls `playedAutoClose()` before the 'free' fallback, and hides an unpinned peek (`hidePlayedSoon`) unless the pointer is on the tray or its tab. The tally handler and the card menu's combat start use `pinPlayedAuto`. **Seat spots** — `L4_SEAT.topY = 132`: the top row's globe top (was `T.y + L4_SEAT.m` = 25); the lower row unchanged (cards end at 404). Five seats: (26,132) (385,132) (933,132) (933,341) (26,341); two: (385,132) (385,341). At 1366 × 768 an empty Played tray ends 20 px above the top-centre globe's circle. **Other seats on the felt** — `clearL4Globes` no longer removes `#l4oppWrap` (an absolute layer at z 50; `renderL4OppCards` empties it whenever the free board is not showing); `renderL4Globes` ends with `updateL4Opponents()` (other globes from their pubs, cards reconciled); `glob.off` = not mine and `online===false` → `.l4goff` (greyed circle, 'offline' below, a tooltip). **Riders** — `pubRiders(cards)`: ids with a `host`, plus ids in the `attached` list of a card without host in ready/torpor/uncontrolled. `renderL4OppCards`, `buildMat` and `renderFull` draw no rider on its own and draw every host's `attached` on it, torpor and uncontrolled included, skipping ids whose card is not in ready / torpor / uncontrolled (offsets: ADX/ADY on the free board and for ready, CX/CY in the Structured rows). `restoreFromPub` re-attaches riders to hosts in any of the three zones. `releaseChildren` and the card menu's Detach run `freeFaceSync` on the freed card. **Harness** exposes `freeFaceZone`, `freeFaceSync`, `freeFaceSyncAll`, `freeDragKeepsZone`, `parkWhere`, `tap`, `unlockedMinions`, `readyToCanon`, `advanceTurnCounter`, `turnLabel`, `tnAdopt`, `openingXfMalus`, `pinPlayed`, `pinPlayedAuto`, `closePlayed`, `togglePlayed`, `avrRender`, `playedAutoPin`, `pubRiders`, `updateL4Opponents`.

> **13 October 2026, afternoon (THE FREE BOARD, READ FOR THE BOTS — a review, no code; client v2.6.148 / server v2.6.44 / bot v0.6.198 unchanged):** reference facts the review established. **`zone` on the free board today** — set by gesture, not by what the card is: a crypt card drawn by double-click on the pile or placed by the table setup is `uncontrolled`; one dragged out of the pile, or dragged anywhere on the felt, is `ready` (`dropZone` returns `'ready'` for the whole felt in L4; the face is kept by `readyDropFace`); a flip (double-click `tap()`, card menu, `groupFlipUp`, `flipSelected`, the owner's `ctrl` flip) changes `faceDown` and never the zone; card menu ▸ To torpor sets `torpor` in place, ▸ Leave torpor does `move(c,'ready',{x:zoneRect('ready').x+24, …})` — the felt's corner in L4; a drag of a torpor card makes it `ready`. **Who reads `zone` on our side:** `unlockedMinions()` (the phase bar's Minion number), `renderStats()` (🧛 for me and for every other seat), `detectClan` / `detectPath` / `clanCount` / `pathCount` (`state.zones.ready`, face up), `bumpCounter`'s transfer rule (`zone === 'uncontrolled' && phase 3`), `undoCryptDraw`, the combat helper's → Torpor button. **The felt's furniture, canonical units:** the hand panel's top edge is y 413 / 414 / 416 at 1366 × 768 / 1536 × 864 / 1920 × 1080; the Played tray is 880 px wide on screen and covers x 131–823, y 24–127 (to 156 with a row of cards) at 1366 × 768, x 175–785, y 22–113 (138) at 1536 × 864, x 244–725, y 20–91 (111) at 1920 × 1080. **The tray and resolvers:** `MP_HANDLERS.tally` calls `pinPlayed()` when a resolver arrives and that helper is on; nothing unpins it when the resolver closes (a pinned tray closes only by its ×, Esc or a click on the tab; `hidePlayedSoon` returns at once while `playedPinned`). **`renderL4()` and the other seats' cards:** `renderL4()` runs `layout()` (which ends in `renderL4OppCards()`) and THEN `renderL4Globes()`, whose `clearL4Globes()` removes `#l4oppWrap`; nothing redraws it until the next `layout()` or `updateL4Opponents()` (a `board` message from another seat). Callers: the `roster` handler (every roster change), `enterL4`, and the resize handler (a second resize listener calls `layout()` right after, so a resize heals itself). **An offline seat** is marked in the Table panel's row ('reconnecting…'), a seat visit's header, a Structured mat's label and the L2 column header; the free board's globe has no mark. **The felt label:** `#l3timer` is written by `updateL3Timer()`, called from `renderL3()` and `renderL4()` only — the `turn` handler re-renders L3 (`net.view === 'ov'`), not L4. **How another seat's cards are drawn (both styles):** every `ready` card without `host` at its own x, y, a vampire's `attached` ids on the vampire; `addRCard` keeps ONE element per seat + id, so a card that is both (the bot's equipment) ends where the LAST call put it. On a Structured mat `ready` x, y are clamped into the ready region and `uncontrolled` / `torpor` are arranged by the mat; on the free board all three zones are drawn at the published x, y on the shared 1004 × 616 felt. **Another seat's Edge on the free board** is drawn only from a `pub.tokens` entry of type `edge` (`renderL4OppCards`); the mats and the Table panel read `pub.edge`. **When a seat stops answering:** the server pings every 30 s and reaps a socket that has been silent for 75 s (`dropPlayer`: '… disconnected.', the roster's `online: false`); the turn stays with that seat; the host's ⏭ Pass their turn… (`hostPass`, only for the seat that holds the turn) moves it on for one round, 💀 Oust this player… for good. A bot on its own Node socket answers pings in JavaScript, so a blocked bot is reaped (run: about 90 s). A browser answers pings by itself: a page whose script is blocked keeps its socket — and the sockets of the lobby bots it hosts — alive, so the table sees everyone online (run: 150 s). A disconnect does not move the host role. **How a bot reaches a Classic table:** the lobby's Add bot up to v2.6.147 (`netBotWhy` read a field the lobby message never carried), a bot on its own socket at any version (the server has no rule); offline a bot seat forces Structured. **The released bot's Classic camp (v0.6.198, read + run):** anchor on an ellipse (502, 300), radii 350 × 180, seat 1 at the top; rows at anchor y − 75 / + 5 / + 85 from x = anchor − 150 in steps of 96, clamped to x 12–908 and y 12–486; distinct slots per row — 7 at the top and bottom seats, 4 at the right-hand seats (5 at seat 3 of 5), 9–11 at the left; `_openSlot` has no bound.

> **13 October 2026, later (THE PIN STANDS — client v2.6.147 / server v2.6.44 / bot v0.6.198 beside the suite; pure client, purge):** **The dock pin** — `#l2dockPin` (a child of `#board`, right after `#l2dockbg`) is an icon button: `aria-label="Keep the panel open"`, `aria-pressed`, a `title` that reads 'Not pinned' / 'Pinned', and ONE inline SVG path (`viewBox 0 0 16 16`, a pushpin drawn point down) as its only content. The click listeners are unchanged (the button toggles; the panel's background pins, and unpins + closes). **Spectators, as the code stands (measured this round, unchanged since at least v2.6.145):** a watcher's client runs the players' paths — `schedulePush` sends `board` with no `net.spect` gate, the server's watcher branch answers every non-chat, non-referee message with `err` 'Spectators can only chat.', `MP_HANDLERS.err` toasts and logs it, and `log` → `scheduleSave` → `schedulePush` sends the next one: an echo at roughly the 600 ms debounce. `joined` and `started` switch no view for a spectator, `#empty` covers the board while `state.cards.size` is 0, the L2 home view is the watcher's own empty board, and `renderL3` / `l3slots` treat `mySeat()` = 0 as a seat (one mat missing in a two-player probe). `switchView(n)` (a seat visit, keys 1–6) is the view that works.

> **13 October 2026 (THE TABLE IS SET — client v2.6.146 / server v2.6.44 / bot v0.6.198 beside the suite; purge the client, restart the server):** **The room option `tableSetup` (server)** — `create` stores `m.boardMode === 'classic' && m.tableSetup === true`; `joinReply`, the spectator's `joined`, `started` and BOTH `took` senders (promote and loadMatch) carry `tableSetup: true` when set — both `took` senders also carry `boardMode` now; `roomSnap` / `roomUnsnap` round-trip it (re-validated: Classic only). `dealNow` is untouched: a Classic deal still sends an empty `unc`. **The option in the client** — `net.tableSetup` (set by `joined` / `started`, kept by `took` when its message has none, reset with the room); `tableSetupOn()` = `freeBoard() && net.tableSetup &&` no non-vacant `bot` in `net.roster`. **Seats on the free board (canonical coordinates)** — `L4_SEAT = { m:10, gap:10, cgap:4, lowBottom:404 }`; `l4SeatGeo()` (card 42 × 59, globe circle 44.8 under an 11.2 name line, seat block 234.8 wide, cards 4.1 below the globe's top); `l4SeatSpot(idx, N)`, pure → the globe's top-left: the top row holds `ceil(N/2)` seats left to right, the lower row the rest right to left; five seats = (26,25) (385,25) (933,25) (933,341) (26,341); the right half puts the globe on the outside. `l4CryptSpots(gx, gy)`, pure → four card spots beside a globe wherever it is (to its right when the block fits on the table, else to its left). `l4SeatOf(seat)` = the seat's position in the WHOLE roster, so an ousted seat moves nobody. `l4SetupSpots(seat)` = beside the globe where it is now, else beside its seat spot. `renderL4Globes` takes the seat spot as the default position when `tableSetupOn()` (the centre ring otherwise) and shows `#l4hint` only without it; `started` and `startHotseat` call `clearL4Globes()` when the option is on, so a new match starts from the spots. **The four cards** — online: `MP_HANDLERS.dealt` calls `l4SetupDealNet(m.crypt)` (stores `net.setupSpots`, sends up to four `drawCrypt`); `MP_HANDLERS.drew` gives each uncontrolled answer that is not a `took` fetch the next stored spot, face down, without the per-card log line. Hotseat / offline: `dealOpening` and `dealOpeningSync(seat)` call `l4SetupDealLocal(seat)` (moves the top four crypt cards, pushes `cryptDrawHistory` so Undo last draw takes them back). `startHotseat(players, boardMode, opts)` reads `opts.tableSetup`; `startOfflineFromCfg` passes `#offSetup`; `hotBridge._tableSetup` mirrors the field in the bridge's `joined`. **The two notes** — `TABLE_SETUP_OLD_SRV_NOTE`: the host ticked the box and the server dropped the field (`net._createSetup` read once in `mpCreate`, checked in `joined` → toast, `net._setupMissed`, and the line again after the deal line). `TABLE_SETUP_BOT_NOTE`: after the deal line at a table with a bot. Both are written AFTER the deal line because `dealt` clears the log. **The dialogs** — Create game → Game settings: `#mpSetupRow` / `#mpSetup` / `#mpSetupDesc` (shown in Lite too; `#mpSetupRow{grid-column:auto}` so the description takes the second cell). Play offline: `#offSetupRow` / `#offSetup`. `tableSetupSync()` greys and disables each box while its board-style select is not Classic; it is called from `mpFoldSync`, both selects' change listeners and `syncOffBotLock` (which sets the select in code). **The globe label** — `ordinal(n)` (1st, 2nd, 3rd, 4th, 11th–13th, 21st); the chip is the chair + the ordinal (`.l4gseat`), on every free-board table. **The dock pin** — `#l2dockPin` is a child of `#board`, right after `#l2dockbg` (it was inside `#z-hand`), absolutely placed in the strip above the dock's boxes at the right; labels `Pin` / `Pinned`; `#l2dockbg.pinned` is a 1 px inset gold line and a faint shadow. `setDockPin` is still the only setter. Harness exposes `ordinal`, `tableSetupOn`, `l4SeatSpot`, `l4CryptSpots`, `l4SetupSpots`, `l4SeatGeo`, `L4_SEAT`, `pubBox`, `l4Globes`, `renderL4Globes`, `mySeat`, `freeBoard`. **Old behaviour around it, measured this round and unchanged:** every room the client creates has the tournament lock, so online `dealt` arrives once per seat per match (redeal and a mid-game deck load are refused). A mid-game `joined` switches no view: `net.view` stays null until Esc / Home. `#empty` (z 80, opaque) is displayed whenever `state.cards.size` is 0 — a spectator's free-board table is underneath it. `restoreFromPub` restores positions for `ready` cards only and scales them by the live board. `serializeGame` holds no globe position. `netBotWhy` tests `net.lobbyData.boardMode`, a key the server's lobby message has never had.

> **12 October 2026, night (THE TABLE LISTENS — client v2.6.143 / server v2.6.43 / bot v0.6.198 beside the suite; pure client, purge):** **The card index** — `processCardData` now calls the pure `buildCardIndex(list)` → `{ map, info, imgNames }`. Both parsers return `{ meta, typesArr, names, imgName, adv, claims }`: `names` = every name the card answers to (v3 now includes `name_variants`, strings or `{name}` objects), `claims` = the strong subset (v3: `name` + variants; v5: variants, plus `printed_name` when the card has no suffix), `adv` = `cardIsAdv(card)` (v3 `adv:true` or a name ending `ADV)`; v5 a suffix containing `ADV`). Ownership of a key: strong claims always write; a weak name writes only when the key is free, or when a base card displaces an advanced card's weak hold. > **12 October 2026, later (THE BADGE KNOWS THE HOLD — client v2.6.142 / server v2.6.43 / bot v0.6.198 beside the suite; purge the client + `/elysium-bot.js`):** the bot project built the Hold-on principle in v0.6.198 (`HANDOFF-to-main-2026-10-12-v0_6_198.md`). **The bots' rule:** a HUMAN's Hold on (say 0, or a chat line matching `/^hold on\b/i`) freezes every wait a bot makes on a human — the block and reaction questions, the pool question, the vote poll, every combat step and the damage step, Turn-based's reminder and give-up (`_holdFreeze` / `_freezeWait`); a release (say 1, 2, 3, 4 or 7, or a chat line matching `/^(carry on|resume|it resolves)\b/i`) from ANY non-bot human clears the whole `_holdBy` set and starts each wait again from the beginning (`_releasePauses`, `_holdThaw`); `_releaseDeparted` drops a holder who is out, vacant or `online: false`. `status()` reports a live hold first (`held` / `release-hold`); a paused poll reports `resolve-vote`. Each bot keeps its OWN `_holdBy`, so the panel's Release hold (`step()`) frees one bot; a bot's own 'Hold on…' is the thinking announce of its react window and holds nothing. **The client's half (v2.6.142):** the hold badge (`hotHoldBy`) mirrors that rule. `hotHoldMark(who, i, txt)` is the one path (a quick-phrase index or a chat line; bot speakers ignored through `hotHoldIsBot`, the roster `bot` flag); `hotHoldPrune()` runs from the `roster` handler and `hotRosterPush`; `hotHoldAfterPanelRelease()` runs when the panel's step returns `did: 'release-hold'` and clears the badge once no bot in this tab reports `held`. The chat line's send path is `chatSendLine(v)` (extracted from the `#chatIn` keydown listener; offline it marks the hold), and `MP_HANDLERS.chat` marks it online. Online, the badge's Resume button is still reserved for the holder (main's choice, open for Johan); the tooltip for another player's hold says anyone can say It resolves. Bot settings shows `BOTCFG_HOLD` unless the loaded bot is older than v0.6.198 (`botCfgHoldLine`). PROTOCOL §12.1 carries 'The hold (index 0)', and §8 `botcfg` points there. **Known limits:** online, after the host's panel Release hold, the other clients' badges clear only at the next release phrase (the panel release is in-process, nothing on the wire); with two bots, the panel frees one bot per press. **Beside the suite now:** `elysium-bot.js` v0.6.198; all three `.js` twins from the bot project's 12 Oct `bundle/` (the cardfx twin for the first time — their 10 Oct zip never carried it); the three JSONs unchanged; START-HERE-BOTS.html (12 Oct, embedded). Tests — client 248 with the bot beside, no skips.

(4) **Undirected actions:** Hunt and Political action flagged, a new 'Undirected card action' row (`takes an undirected action.`); the BLOCKER opens the Block counter (it is their 'Block!'), so the ACTOR's client writes stealth 1 onto an untouched 0 when a block opens against its undirected action (`blockSeedUndir`, from avrOpen and the MP handler) — my first version seeded it on the actor's OWN open, which never happens; the first test passed vacuously (the harness boots in `free` mode) until I checked why. > **23 September 2026 (CROWS NEED A STRIKE RESOLUTION — client v2.6.136 / server v2.6.41 / bot v0.6.166 beside the suite; purge the client (supersedes the never-deployed v2.6.135), restart the server for srv-2.6.41):** Johan's 'anything before the handover?' on v2.6.135. (1) **Every-round damage after combat ends:** Carrion Crows-class damage lands during the NORMAL strike resolution — a combat-ends strike ends combat before it (VEKN forum rulings 72008, 74854, 60259; a dodge does not stop it) — yet v2.6.135 counted it after combat ends, in the taken line the bot reads and in the resolve total. `cmbEnvOf`: a combat-ends strike in the FIRST pair cancels that round's every-round damage; in a later pair it already landed. (2) **Found by the new test — a dodge depended on click ORDER:** the dodger's client zeroed the opponent's damage only when the opponent picked AFTER it through the client; a bot writing its strike damage after the human's dodge (the acting side picks first) still reached the taken line. `cmbDodged` makes a first-pair dodge a count-time rule (it stops the opposing strike, normal and aggravated, never the every-round damage). (3) **One damage function:** the taken line, the read-out, the history and the totals all read `cmbSideDamage` / `cmbDealt` (the taken line had its own sum). (4) The stage names the combatants ('Each round: Lisa 1 · Two 0'). Johan: the Combat menu (13 rows) gets trimmed later if it feels long. Bot addendum §6. Gates: client 227/0 with bot v0.6.166 (the new test red on v2.6.135; one v2.6.134 test re-ordered — it had a dodge BEFORE the bot's 4 and expected the 4 to land), dispatch 53/0, protocol 6/0, canary 37/0, error-endpoint 21/1 (cardfx.json not beside), B1 byte-identical, check-versions green.

> **23 September 2026 (AGGRAVATED, WOUNDED AND EVERY ROUND — client v2.6.135 / server v2.6.41 / bot v0.6.166 beside the suite; purge the client AND restart the server (supersede the never-deployed v2.6.134 / srv-2.6.40)):** Johan's picks from 'what else would help humans and bots navigate combat?'. **(1) Step-by-step 'done':** no code — the resolver row's Pass already sends the `Pass` quick phrase, which bot v0.6.165+ takes as 'nothing more in this step' (Johan: use what is established). **(2) Aggravated damage:** `Combat ▸ Aggravated ▸ 1–4, N…` (`strikes for N aggravated damage.`); new tally fields `ga`/`gb` (srv-2.6.41, per round); `cmbDamage(inc, prevented, blood, agg)` follows VEKN 4 Damage Resolution — prevention on aggravated first, normal mended with blood (unmended → wounded), the first unprevented aggravated point wounds without blood, each further point costs 1 blood or the vampire is BURNED (checked against the rulebook text and a forum ruling: Crows 2 on 1 blood + 1 aggravated = burned); the taken line gains ' and is burned'. **(3) Wounded ends the combat:** `cmbWounded` (from Damage on) skips Press and makes End of round's Next end the combat even after a press to continue; a wounded vampire of yours gets '→ Torpor' (pushUndo + move — the player decides). **(4) Every-round damage** (`Combat ▸ Damage each round ▸`, Carrion Crows-class, `ea`/`eb`, kept by a new round, cannot be dodged, 0 clears); **history** (`_cmbHist`, local, from the same tally on every screen) in the stage; the resolve line `Combat: A vs B damage dealt.` keeps its shape but sums the whole combat (`cmbTotals`). **Found on the way:** (a) a vampire this screen cannot see had blood 0 → 'wounded' → the combat ended — unknown blood is now null, never wounded; (b) stepping back into Damage re-snapshotted the ALREADY-BURNED blood → a healthy vampire looked wounded — one snapshot per round; (c) the client's own 'Round 2 ▸' carried a private copy of the new-round rule that forgot Carrion Crows — `cmbNewRound` now calls `tallyCombatMerge` itself. (1) **A lost taken line:** `avrPhaseTo` pushes through the 120 ms `avrPush` debounce, but `cmbNewRound` sends at once and clears it — another seat clicking Damage → Press → End → Round 2 quickly reaches the other screens as `ph 4` then `rd 2`, and `cmbWatch` only counted a step past Damage within the SAME round (reproduced: zero lines). (3) **Bot gap (their fix):** the released bot writes the combat tally only at its announce (`a:0, b:0`), so against a bot the Damage step reads *Incoming 0* and no taken line is written unless the human corrects with ±; main does NOT parse bot strike lines into the counter (a second truth that would double-count later) — item 0 of the bot spec, together with dropping `a:0, b:0` from their announce. **Johan's decisions:** a player with the combat helper off keeps menu = log lines only (coordination by chat / quick phrases); combat ends in a later strike pair keeps the earlier pairs' damage (play resolves damage per pair — VEKN forum 82464 and the rulebook's 'no longer ready' clause; documented at the site). +1 test (red on v2.6.129). > **23 September 2026 (THE COMBAT STEPS CARRY STATE — client v2.6.129 / server v2.6.39 / bot v0.6.162 beside the suite; purge the client AND restart the server):** Johan's step-driven combat helper, rulebook-checked against VEKN's rulebook (4. Detailed Turn Sequence). **The steps stay the rulebook's seven** (`AVR_PHASES` was already exactly right; Johan's list had Additional strikes as a step and no Damage resolution — additional strikes are extra choose/resolve pairs INSIDE Strike, damage is its own step), so `ph` keeps its meaning on the wire. **Panel:** in combat the `#avrLive` row becomes a clickable rail (`cmbRail`: R#, seven step chips, range pill; the a/b counters move out via the new `#avrCnt` wrapper), and `#avrDuel` gets a STAGE between the two combatants (`cmbStage`: Set range this/next round, the Close/Long switch, damage a vs b with ±, Next strike pair, the Ends/Continues switch, the outcome) plus per-side controls in each panel (`cmbPanelCtl`: Maneuver, Strike ▾ / + Additional, the Damage read-out Incoming · prevent ± · Burns X → Y left / wounded → torpor, Press). `avrPhase` follows the rules: a set range (`rl`) skips Determine range, a combat-ends strike (`ce`) jumps to End of round, End of round's Next = `Round N ▸` (uncancelled press to continue → `cmbNewRound`) or `End combat` (→ `avrResolve`); ◂ no longer crosses back into the previous round. A lone combatant who is not the turn seat now waits on the RIGHT (`cmbSidesIn`). **Menu:** the ready minion's right-click menu gets `Combat` right after Actions (`cmbMenu`: Maneuver to X, Set range ▸ this/next round, Strike ▸ six, Additional strike…, Prevent damage…, Press to continue/end) — same functions as the panel, `noUndo`, no lock, no batch close. A control used with no combat open opens one and announces that vampire (`cmbEnsure`); with the helper off only the log line goes out. **Lines:** `CMB_LINE` builds every line; strike / steal / dodge / combat-ends / maneuver / press / taken are the bot's own shapes (verified against the released bot's L12 + `_parseCombatNarration` in a `testB`), `sets range to X[ next round]`, `gets an additional strike (CARD)`, `prevents N damage (CARD)` are new (PROTOCOL §12). **Damage is never applied to the board** (Johan: the players keep track). Passing Damage resolution (or resolving from Strike/Damage) makes EACH client log its OWN combatant's `takes N (loses P blood, now M)[ and goes to torpor].` once per round (`cmbWatch` → `cmbLogTaken`, key serial:round:seat), from the blood snapshot taken on ENTERING Damage (`cmbSnapNow`) — a player who burns blood mid-step does not skew it; × withdraw never logs. **Wire:** combat tally fields `rng rl nx pr sp ce sa sb xa xb pa pb`; the human client sends them as PARTIAL writes (`cmbSet` → `cmbSend`) beside the unchanged whole-state `avrPush` (a/b/ph/rd). srv-2.6.39 `tallyCombat` merges them (absent = preserve, a changed `rd` = fresh round with `nx` carried into `rng` + `rl` 1, mode change = fresh, only combat broadcasts carry them); the client's `tallyCombatMerge` (MP handler + `hotBridge.tallyIn`) is the twin, and a suite test compares both on 400 random writes (mutant-checked). `a`/`b` are now per ROUND. `avrDuelAnnounce` online writes the own seat optimistically. `avrFresh(mode)` replaces the six `avr={…}` literals. **Gates:** client 217/0 with bot v0.6.162 beside (+8 tests; the 7 combat ones red on v2.6.128; the stale v2.6.126 LIVE test fixed — it listened on `chat`, the bot's notes ride `log` since v0.6.114), dispatch 51/0 (+1, red on srv-2.6.38), protocol 6/0 (the lint now follows `tallyCombat(m`/`tallyCombatMerge(m` to their SOURCE key lists — red with the old doc), canary 37/0, error-endpoint 21/1 (cardfx.json not beside — expected), B1 byte-identical 1 438 501 (built to /tmp/rebuilt.html — see learnings), check-versions green. **Not done: a browser look** (stage width 190 px, rail wrap in a narrow overlay). **Bot follow-up:** `spec-bot-combat-fields-2026-09-23.md`. Architecture note: the combat helper is `cmb*` (one block before the duel view); state = the tally (a/b/ph/rd + the twelve combat fields), rendering = `cmbRail` / `cmbStage` / `cmbPanelCtl` rebuilt per render, the only local-only state is `_cmbSerial` / `_cmbSnap` / `_cmbDone` (declared early, TDZ).

# Elysium - Project Context & Working Reference

> **22 September 2026, latest (BOT-CARD CONTROLS AT A HOTSEAT + BURN — client v2.6.126 / server v2.6.38 / bot v0.6.151, pure client, purge):** the bot project's handover spec implemented. At a hotseat, every card verb on a bot's board (blood/blue/green ±1, lock/unlock, flip, torpor/untorpor) now travels `hotCtrl` → `hotBridge.toBot(seat, {t:'ctrl', cid, act, from})` — the server's shape — and the bot's next publish is the authority (the v2.6.97 seat-verb pattern, now for cards). Before: `hotCtrl`'s placeholder-blob and `owner` guards returned silently for a bot seat — menu open, click dead, bot never told. New: a `🔥 Burn (remove from game)` row on a bot's card menu, both modes, behind a confirm — the bot's `burn` receiver (≥ v0.6.129) finally has a sender; a human who diablerises a bot's torpored vampire has the verb. `ctrlOptimistic` is the shared optimistic write (burn included). Human seats untouched. One sync test (fake bridge) + one LIVE `testB` (real bot; NOT RUN here — no bot file in the session; run before deploy). Harness exports `hotCtrl`/`ctrlOptimistic`. Protocol doc rows for `ctrl` mention `burn`; no wire change, no new bot floor. Handover back: bot project re-runs H1–H9 + canary, documents Burn, closes debt 26h.

> **21 September 2026, latest (THE SOAK WITH THE DATABASE — no code change; client v2.6.125 / server v2.6.38 / bot v0.6.151):** Johan uploaded `elysium-cardfx.json` (kept in the bot project), so the one thing the tool had not proven — its own database path — is proven. `--control` exit 0 with `cardfx database: yes`. **Full battery, ~7 min: 7 tables · 91 turns · 213 bot Hold-ons delivered · 9 ousts · 0 STUCK · exit 0.** Every 2-bot table played to last-standing (3–7 rounds); the 4-bot table ousted three seats over 9 rounds in 30 turns — fewer than 36, i.e. the sweep skipped fallen seats (spec item 1) with the database on, through the real `hotRunBotTurns`. This is the first end-to-end evidence of the oust path (bounty → roster → skipped seat) in a whole game on our side; the earlier 731-Hold-on battery stays as the no-database complement. Nothing was ousted wrongly (each table ended with exactly one live seat). `elysium-cardfx.json` stays out of our delivery (bot project owns it).

> **21 September 2026 (A SOAK THAT CAN FAIL — new tool `elysium-soak-hotseat.js` v1.0.0; client v2.6.125 / server v2.6.38 / bot v0.6.151 all UNCHANGED, nothing to deploy):** the one-off script that justified removing the Hold-on filter, made a project tool on Johan's word ("mer verktyg som underlättar är bra"). **What it runs:** real `Bot` instances on the REAL `hotBridge`, driven round after round by the REAL `hotRunBotTurns` through the harness — the question the unit suite cannot ask: does an all-bot table keep MOVING? (A relay bug here is rarely a wrong value; it is a table that parks — round 3, v2.6.95.) `node elysium-soak-hotseat.js` = the default battery (six 2-bot tables at pool 30/14 + one 4-bot); `--decks a,b --pool --rounds` for one table; `--idle`, `--persona`, `--client`, `--bot`; uses `elysium-cardfx.json` when it sits beside the tool and SAYS so when it does not. Exit 0 moved / 2 stuck / 3 cannot run. Exports `runTable`, `loadDeps`, `DEFAULT_BATTERY`. **Two things make its green mean something, both built because I had named them as gaps an hour earlier:** (1) **`--control`** seats a bot that never hears its own turn and DEMANDS that the trip-wire fires — it is also a `testB` in the client suite, so the control runs at every gate with the bot beside. (2) **An independent watchdog.** The soak detects a parked seat through `hotAwaitPass`'s idle clock — the very mechanism under test; with that clock broken the first version simply HUNG. The watchdog keeps its own last-heard stamp (wrapping `_fromBot`, deliberately NOT `rec.lastAt`) and races the sweep at 3× the idle window. Proven with a MUTANT client (`hotAwaitPass`'s comparison replaced by `return;`): the control exits 2 in seconds with *CLIENT FAULT: only this tool's watchdog noticed* instead of hanging. Three distinct verdicts: fired / client fault / nothing fired. **Small honesty fixes on the way:** my first exit-code check measured `tail`, not the tool (a pipe) — re-measured unpiped: unknown deck 3, no bot file 3, control 0; a usage mistake now prints a sentence, not a stack (`SOAK_STACK=1` for the stack). **Limits, printed by the tool itself:** no human seat, no DOM; without the database nobody is ousted, so the oust path is NOT exercised (the suite's LIVE tests cover it in the small). It is not the bot project's `elysium-bot-hotseat.js` — that one LIFTS the bridge and imitates the sweep, this one runs the sweep; quote both. Keep `--idle` well under the bots' 30 s chat heartbeat or a parked table looks alive. **First full battery with the finished tool** (client v2.6.125, bot v0.6.151, no database, ~13 min): 7 tables · 184 turns · 731 bot Hold-ons delivered to another bot · 0 ousts · **0 STUCK** · exit 0. (Seen, not chased: in every 2-bot table the fanned tallies were exactly 2× the Hold-ons — one tally broadcast per Hold-on, counted once per receiving seat; consistent with plain play where the reactor never writes an intercept.) In the sandbox a plain `nohup … &` dies with the tool call — detach with `setsid` (SKILLS recipe). Registered in `check-versions.js` (present/MISSING) and PROJECT-MANIFEST §B. Gates: client 203/0 (+1 `testB`), check-versions green; code under test untouched, so dispatch / protocol / canary / B1 stand from v2.6.125.

> **21 September 2026 (THE LAST DIFFERENCE — client v2.6.125 / server v2.6.38 / bot v0.6.151, pure client — purge client + `/elysium-bot.js` together; srv-2.6.38 unchanged):** the bot project's `reply-to-main-parity-2026-09-21.md`, reviewed by RUNNING it. **Their results on our release:** `--arm asis` `droppedVerbs {}` · `tallyNet 0` (the acceptance is met), gate H1–H10 10/0, the lift works unchanged, canary 37/0. **Bot v0.6.151 consumed** (canary 37/0, client suite green with it). Both handover findings were real and are fixed: **B1** `_onGiven` now matches `attachTo` by card id first — verified LIVE here through our own path (a real bot's published vampire id == its board id; `given{attachTo:<that cid>}`: v0.6.150 arms nothing, v0.6.151 arms `_famedMine`; the fx lookup was stubbed because the sandbox has no cardfx database, so what was measured is the host match). Their own fixture had the wrong wire shape baked in (`attachTo:'Ayelech'`) — that is how it stayed green: the 76-lesson again, on their side. **B2** `_oust()` sends `bounty` before the pool-0 board; through v2.6.124 their net now reads 0 ousts / 0 self-collected. **§D — the filter is removed.** `_fromBot case 'say'` relays every phrase incl. a bot's `i:0`, the server's relay exactly. Evidence: their read of both consumers of `i===0` + 9 tables with the line cut from OUR source (349 delivered, 0 stuck); our soak `/tmp/soak.js` = real bots through the real `hotRunBotTurns` with an 8 s idle trip-wire: 7 tables (six 2-bot pairings at pool 30 / 14, one 4-bot), 695 bot Hold-ons delivered to another bot, **0 stuck**, every table well past round 3. *Limit of OUR soak: no cardfx database here, so the bots play plain and nobody was ousted; theirs ran with the database to last-standing. The two are complementary, neither alone is the proof.* **Two things became load-bearing and are pinned:** (1) `rosterMsg()` must keep `bot` — the bot's filter is `players[].bot` and a roster push REPLACES its players, so dropping the flag brings the round-3 deadlock back at the first oust; (2) the bot-file floor is REAL: `hotBotVersionOk()` / `HOT_BOT_MIN='0.6.147'` in `hotLoadBotRes`, checked on EVERY load (an old global stays defined), readable reason in both spawn dialogs. Before, only the V5 keys failed readably; `hacking`/`sewer` spawned on any file. **Contracts recorded from their reply:** (a) their tool IMITATES our sweep's who-is-out decision (it follows `hotBridge._out`; we read `net.roster` — kept equal by `syncRoster` at every writer). **If the sweep's out-rule changes, tell them.** (b) C3: a bot is never legitimately silent > 3 min without a hold — a 30 s `⌛ Still thinking…` chat heartbeat; `rec.lastAt` is stamped for EVERY typed verb incl. `chat` (checked), so the idle dialog means a fault. (c) C5, accepted as the better behaviour: with *Pool to predator* OFF a human's oust sends no `bounty`, and offline the bot's no-server net (keyed on `joined.srv`) still reads the pool-0 board and collects — online it would do neither. (d) B3: they KEEP the self-pass (it ends `hotAwaitPass` for a bot ousted on its own turn offline). (e) B4 un-oust: open by Johan's ruling — an un-ousted bot stays out and passes; not a bridge fault. (f) `wall` stays hidden. The OPEN-WITH-THE-BOTS note below is hereby closed. Gates: client 202/0 with bot v0.6.151 (+1 test, 1 rewritten; destructive 2/2 vs the DELIVERED v2.6.124), dispatch 50/0, protocol 6/0, canary 37/0, B1 byte-identical 1309541, check-versions green.

> **21 September 2026 (AN OUST PAYS THE LIVING AND MOVES THE TURN — client v2.6.123 / server v2.6.38 / bot v0.6.150, SERVER RESTART + purge; ships together with client v2.6.123 and bot v0.6.150):** the two server items of the bot project's 21 Sep spec, taken at Johan's word so online and offline close in ONE deploy. **(1) The live predator.** `bounty` and `hostOust` took `(i - 1 + n) % n` without asking whether that seat was still in the game. Shown LIVE before the fix (real server, three real bots): seat 2 out, seat 3 falls → `B got bounty from C`, roster `2:out/vp1`, the real predator's pool +0. After: `A got bounty from C`, `vp2`, +6. `prevLiveSeat()` skips `out` and deliberately NOT `vacant` — a vacant seat is still in the game. Nobody left → no VP, and the sys line no longer names a recipient. **(2) The turn.** An oust of the seat that HOLDS the turn hands it on (`turnOnFromOusted` → `nextLiveSeat`, broadcast AFTER the roster so clients learn why before what), exactly as `vacateSeat` has always done. **The protocol doc already said `bounty` "advances the turn if needed"** — the doc promised it and the server never did; the lint cannot see that (it diffs names, not behaviour). **(3) Found by running it:** with (2) in place the ousted bot's own late `pass` (bot v0.6.140+) drew `err: It is not your turn.` into every such trace. A pass from a seat that is `out` and does not hold the turn is now ignored silently; a LIVE seat passing out of turn still errs (v2.5.64). The turn is never advanced twice — asserted in the unit test and seen live (`C saw turn -> [2]`). **Probe hygiene, relearned:** the server writes `elysium-rooms.json` beside ITSELF (`DATA_DIR = __dirname`), so a live probe leaves a snapshot in the workdir and the next run dies on `That room name is taken` — delete both json files between runs (CLAUDE.md has said so since June) and never let them into a delivery. And `pkill -f <port>` kills the shell whose command line contains that port. > **21 September 2026 (THE ORDER WAS LUCK — client v2.6.123 / server v2.6.37 / bot v0.6.150, pure client — purge; supersedes v2.6.122, deploy this one):** Johan's standing "anything odd?" gate, answered by re-reading the diff and RUNNING the one path the v2.6.122 suite had only driven with fake seats. **(1) The host-oust order was the opposite of what the comment said.** `hostOustHot` told the bot first (`forceOust`), and the bot's `send()` → `_drain()` is SYNCHRONOUS while its token bucket has tokens, so its pool-0 board fanned out inside that call: the predator saw `board > oust-inferred > bounty > roster`. The +6 came out right only because bot v0.6.147's no-server net records the name and swallows the verb that follows — correctness borrowed from the other project's safety net, under a comment and three doc entries claiming we had ordered it ourselves. Now `awardBountyHot` + `hotRosterPush` run BEFORE `forceOust`; measured live: `bounty > roster > board`, no inference. The v2.6.122 notes below are corrected by this entry: the 'synchronously ahead of the board' claim held for a HUMAN's oust (600 ms debounce) and not for a bot's. **(2) A give to an ousted bot destroyed the card.** The bot's `given` receiver returns early when `out`; `giveHotToBot` sent the verb and destroyed our copy regardless. Refused with a toast now, before anything is touched. **(3) gid was cut at 16** — the tally duel cid's cap, copied onto the wrong verb; the server's `cleanId` strips `[^a-zA-Z0-9_-]` and keeps 32. **(4) The registry promise was half kept:** the bot file loaded only at Start / Add bot, so a deck the client has never heard of could not appear in a menu until after the first spawn. `hotBotMenuPreload()` loads it quietly when a seat becomes a bot and when the host's lobby renders; failures stay silent there because the spawn path owns the dialog (and the loader forgets a failed attempt). **Looked at and left:** the idle dialog stays open if the bot passes while it is showing (its buttons are then no-ops — `fin` is once-only; `uiDialog` has no programmatic close); `hotBotOusted` recomputes the predator from `net.roster` rather than trusting the bridge's `pred` argument (all five `out` writers call `hotRosterPush`, census: 7147/7205/7217/7258/8300, so the two books cannot disagree today); a bot's `res:1` tally runs the human's own Resolve-clear setting, as online. Gates: client 201/0 with the bot (+2; destructive 2/2 vs the DELIVERED v2.6.122), canary 37/0, dispatch 47/0, protocol 6/0, B1 byte-identical 1307218, check-versions green.

> **21 September 2026 (THE PARITY ROUND — client v2.6.122 / server v2.6.37 / bot v0.6.150, pure client — purge; bundle bot v0.6.150 + the re-issued START-HERE-BOTS.html):** the bot project's handover `spec-hotseat-parity-2026-09-21.md`, items 1–6 (client parts) + the gate it asked for. Measured by them THROUGH our bridge: 4 of 4 tables froze on an ousted bot, 0 stealth cards in 35 block attempts, no bot ever learned anybody left the game. **The design move:** `hotBridge` plays the SERVER's role, so it now keeps the server's three pieces of table state ITSELF — `tally` (room.tally), `_out`, `_vp` — with `tallyIn()`, `predOf()`, `rosterMsg()/pushRoster()/syncRoster()`. Self-contained on purpose: their harness lifts the `hotBridge` literal + `hotFanOut` VERBATIM, and a relay that needed `net.roster` or a client function would silently not run there (verified: the lifted literal relays tally/bounty/roster/openHand/give with nothing else defined). Everything client-shaped is `typeof`-guarded (`hotBotOusted`, `hotGivenToHuman`, `MP_HANDLERS`). **(1) The sweep** skips roster-`out` bots (also ones ousted DURING the sweep: no toast, no camera, no turn) and `hotAwaitPass()` replaces the bare await: an IDLE clock (`HOT_PASS_IDLE_MS` 3 min from the bot's last bridge message `rec.lastAt`; stands still while anyone holds) that ASKS — it never advances by itself, because a bot is legitimately silent while a human resolves a combat by hand and a Hold has no timer by design; Cancel re-arms. The same exit is `⏭ Pass their turn…` on the seat menu offline (`hotForcePass`), the twin of `hostPass`. A table torn down mid-sweep leaves the promise unresolved on purpose. **(2) tally:** `GAME_HANDLERS.tally` field for field (absent fields preserved while the mode is unchanged, mode change fresh, duel merged per seat, `res` relayed never stored), to every bot seat INCLUDING the sender and into `MP_HANDLERS.tally` for the human; the human's `avrPush`, both clear/resolve sends and the duel announce go through `hotTallySend()` as `who:<human>` (not echoed back into the handler — it would re-run a resolve's clear). All self-gated on `hotBotsSeated()`: a human-only hotseat is what it was. **(3) bounty + roster:** the bridge's `bounty` case marks out, pays the LIVE predator and pushes the roster; `awardBountyHot` pays where the pool LIVES — the verb to a bot, **the live state to the ACTIVE human (a pre-existing loss: the active seat's blob is stale while it plays, so a host-oust bounty to the device holder vanished at the next hand-off; with one human + bots it is the normal case)**, the blob (+ pub) to a stored human. `hotRosterPush()` at all five `out` sites, synchronously AHEAD of the pool-0 board so bot v0.6.147's no-server net sees `out` already set; the other order (bot self-oust: board first) is covered by the bot's own de-duplication — the LIVE test asserts +6 exactly once. **(4) decks:** `HOT_BOT_DECKS` is `{key,label}` over `ElysiumBot.ARCHETYPES`; both spawn paths pass `deckName`; + V5 Ventrue; the three embedded V5 lists (list-identical, checked) are gone; unknown registry keys appear under the deck's own name once the bot is loaded; `wall` hidden (retired 4 Aug). Selection is stored by KEY (`botDeckKey`, `#lobBotDeck` values). Needs bot ≥ v0.6.147 beside the client — `hotBotDeckFor` throws a readable reason into both spawn-failure dialogs. **(5) levels:** `HOT_BOT_PERSONAS` expert/experienced/novice, `HOT_BOT_PERSONA='expert'` at all four fallback sites, the toggle's first click lands on Expert, lobby select values AND labels. **(6) relays:** `openHand` (+revoke) to the human's receiver and the other bots; `give` → the server's whitelisted `given` + `gave`; human → bot `giveHotToBot` (no undo entry: the bot has no `recall` receiver). **Not in this round (server, needs a restart → srv-2.6.38):** `GAME_HANDLERS.bounty`/`hostOust` take the ring predecessor without skipping `out` seats; the turn is not handed on when its holder is ousted. **For the bot project (read, not run):** `_onGiven` matches `attachTo` against the vampire's NAME, the wire carries a CID (srv 863 / client `given`) — a human's Fame/Pentex drop probably never arms its hooks online either. A bot that ousted itself has no un-oust receiver. Gates: client 199/0 with the bot beside (+9 tests; destructive 9/9 vs v2.6.121 — eight fail, and the sweep test reproduces THE FREEZE: the pristine client never returns), canary 37/0, dispatch 47/0, protocol 6/0, endpoint 21/0 (+1: cardfx.json not beside), B1 byte-identical 1 305 927, check-versions green. Browser check before purge: 1 human + 2 bots to an oust (Played-tab counters moving on a bot's block, +6 on your own oust of a bot, the round continuing), the Debug hand offline, the six decks and three levels in both lobbies.

> **17 September 2026 (ENTER STAYS WITH THE FOCUSED CONTROL — client v2.6.119 / server v2.6.37 / bot v0.6.115, pure client — purge):** Johan's standing "anything odd?" gate on v2.6.118, before deploy. `chatFocus` (Enter) was gated only on the chat line being visible — so a player who had tabbed to or clicked a button (Menu, Pass, ▶) and pressed Enter to activate it would have had the key prevented and the caret sent to the chat instead. `chatFocusWanted()` is the gate now: chat line visible AND no button/link/select/summary/label holds focus; a bare focus (body / the felt) still hands Enter to the chat.
