> **17 September 2026 (FIVE FROM THE FEEDBACK ROUND — client v2.6.118 / server v2.6.37 / bot v0.6.115, pure client — purge):** five items from the players' feedback on the clients, all client-side. **(1) Enter focuses the chat line** when it is not already active: a rebindable action `chatFocus` (Shortcuts › Advanced, default Enter) gated by `chatVisible()` — where there is no chat line (solo) the key is not even prevented, the browser keeps it; inside an input the keymap was already silent. **(2) The chat bubble's text is crisp:** `.chatb .saytxt` weight 700, a brighter bone (#f4ecdc) and a tight dark halo instead of the wide red glow; the medallion and the clan mark keep their opacity (a test pins both). The whole-bubble keyframe opacity is unchanged — text and medallion share one element, so the glyphs were sharpened, not un-faded. **(3) One key cycles the phases:** `phaseNext` (default §) → Unlock → … → Discard → Unlock, from -1 it starts at Unlock; it goes THROUGH `activatePhase`, so the leave-phase check and the Unlock prompts apply as for F1–F5. § was the FIXED view-cycle key (with `, a dead key on Swedish keyboards, so § was the Swedish way in). Johan's call (alt. A): the view cycle (`cycleView`, default V) and the Tab flip (`activeHome`, default Tab) became rebindable ACTIONS with `when()` gates (`tableView`) — an inapplicable key falls through to the browser unprevented — and `nav:true`, so view/UI moves are not undo points (the fixed block never pushed undo; the keymap does, and `nav` keeps that true). `keyBindsReconcile(stored)`: a newcomer whose default the player had already bound elsewhere starts unbound — the player's binding wins, the same one-key-one-action rule `captureKey` enforces. The global handler is the extracted `keyDispatch(e)` (returns handled/not), which the suite drives with a plain `{key,target}` event. **(4) Tab never reached a bot offline, and the reason is in `pass()`:** the human's seat stays in `net.turnSeat` for the whole `hotRunBotTurns` sweep (it is written to the NEXT human only after the sweep), so `toggleActiveHome` read active = me and went home on every press. Now the sweep sets the acting bot as `turnSeat` while it plays (`updatePhaseGlow` follows) and restores the human's seat in `finally`, before `pass()` writes the next one. Consequence to know: during a bot's turn the phase bar is read-only (`pcother`) and PREY/PREDATOR count from the bot — bot turns ARE turns (v2.6.67). Online the server's `turn{seat}` already covers bot seats; read as working, not browser-verified this round. **(5) Esc on the L0 start screen painted the felt + pool globe:** the global Esc rung ran `goHome()` unconditionally → `switchView('l2')` → `enterL2()` drops `l0mode`. `goHome` is skipped while `tableIdle()` (the v2.6.79 idle predicate: no cards, no hotseat, no room); overlays and menus still close. The Esc handler is a document listener the harness cannot fire, so this one is a source pin. Gates: client 182/0 + 5 skipped (187 with the bot; +6 tests, destructive 6/6 vs v2.6.117; the v2.6.107 pin on weight 600 updated to 700 — it pins the SIZE), dispatch 47/0, protocol 6/0, endpoint 20/0 (+2 bot statics), canary skipped without the bot, B1 byte-identical 1 282 724, check-versions green. Browser check before purge: Enter in a hotseat with bots and online; § through a turn; V/Tab in a room; Esc on the start card.

> **16 September 2026 (THE PERMANENT RESUME — client v2.6.117 / server v2.6.37 / bot v0.6.115, pure client — purge):** Johan: a Resume on the start card that neither closing the browser nor purging the cache would shift, and clicking it opened a broken table with no room behind it. **The chain:** the `closed` handler (v2.6.105) sets `net.status='off'` BEFORE calling `mpLeave`, on purpose — no `leave` to a room the server has deleted, no 2.5 s rejoin loop. But `mpLeave` decided whether to clear the felt with `hadGame=netGame()`, and `netGame()` is `inRoom() && net.deckUp` — `inRoom()` is `net.status==='in'`. So every table that ended by Close table, or by srv-2.6.36 closing a bot room, took the `else` branch: cards stayed, `net.room` went null, and the next minute-autosave (or `beforeunload`) wrote that online board to `saveKey()` — which without a room is the BARE `elysium.game`, the solo save. Nothing removes that key except the Settings button, localStorage is untouched by a purge, and Resume then restores an online board into a client with no room: the "broken view". **The fix:** `hadGame=!!net.deckUp` — the question is whether an online deal put these cards here, and `deckUp` answers it without consulting the status the caller has just changed. The clear+forget is one function, `mpTableGone(roomKey)`, and the failed-rejoin branch of `err` (status `'re'`, the room is gone) — the same hole, pre-v2.6.105 and rarer — calls it too. **Tests that could not have been source assertions:** two behavioural tests deal a card, seed the per-room save, fire `closed` / `err` through `MP_HANDLERS` and read the storage stub back (`__ls`, new on the harness): felt empty, room key gone, solo key untouched; destructive 2/2 against v2.6.116. **For Johan's stuck button:** one press of Settings › Files & storage › Delete the autosaved game. **Introduced at v2.6.105, caught here** — the ordering fix that made `closed` safe for the socket made it unsafe for the felt; the two facts lived in different functions and no test covered the felt after `closed`. Gates: client 181/0, dispatch 47/0, protocol 6/0, canary 37/0, B1 byte-identical 1309541, check-versions green.

> **16 September 2026 (THE LOCK IS THE DEFAULT — client v2.6.116 / server v2.6.37 / bot v0.6.115, pure client — purge):** Johan, after the v2.6.115 recap of what the lock actually governs: drop the visual toggle, keep the lock on, and bring the choice back only if feedback asks for it. `#mpTournLock` is gone from Create game and `mpCreate` sends `tournLock:true` unconditionally; `mpLiteDefaults` no longer forces a checkbox that does not exist. What the lock governs is unchanged and all of it lives on the server: deck legality on import (12+ crypt, 60–90 library, expanded counts), no deck swap or redeal after start, no VP self-adjust, un-oust host-only, 🏆 in the room list. It is independent of the helper policy's "Tournament mode" — the two share a word and nothing else. The wire key stays, so a pre-v2.6.116 client with the box unticked still creates an unlocked room against the same server (accepted: the choice was never enforced server-side and this is a UI default, not a policy). Tutorial: `oh-tournlock` removed (a stop cannot spotlight a control that is not there), the Helpers stop carries the explanation. **Reversal recipe** in the HTML comment at the label's old place: label + the read in `mpCreate` + the tutorial stop. **Pre-deploy self-review of v2.6.115** (Johan's ask) found one thing to tighten: the Reaction-window cancel on a verb-bearing lock clone also fired on the controller relay's `by <name>` lock (8757) — another player locking your card is not a reaction; excluded. Checked and left alone: the bot's reconnect can never re-seat a bot we made leave (it requires `started`, and the lobby X exists only in the lobby); `send` queues and drains on the bot's own socket; the 2 s confirmation ceiling falls back to the host kick. Gates: client 179/0, dispatch 47/0, protocol 6/0, canary 37/0, B1 byte-identical 1309541, check-versions green.

> **16 September 2026 (THE ECHO, THE STOPWATCH AND THE X — client v2.6.115 / server v2.6.37 / bot v0.6.115, pure client — purge):** three finds from Johan's test round, plus the bot project's 16 Sep handoff read end to end. **(1) The X in the lobby left the bot sitting there.** `netBotRemove` (v2.6.99) sent `kick` on the HOST's socket and closed the BOT's in the same breath, and those two race across the wire. A socket that merely closes is a DISCONNECT, not a departure: `dropPlayer(conn,false)` (srv 1536) keeps the seat in the roster with `p.off` stamped, for the reconnect window a human is entitled to — and `kick` refuses exactly that seat, "still reconnecting — give them a moment" (srv 1027). So the bot both vanished (no socket, shown offline) and stayed (a roster seat nothing could remove); Johan's "hänger kvar men registrerar till viss del att den inte finns kvar" was a precise reading of the state. The bot LEAVES now, on its own socket — the same socket the lobby already uses to claim its seat — and `leave` is `dropPlayer(conn,true)`, which splices the seat out at once. We close only once the lobby push confirms it is gone (2 s ceiling), and the host kick stays as the fallback for a bot whose socket is already dead, where there is no grace window left to refuse it. No server change, and it works against every deployed server. **(2) Your own chat bubbled twice under Show all.** Your line has TWO sources — the send path paints it the instant you press Enter (9141/9145) and the server echoes it back to every client including you. v2.6.50 hid the echo as a SIDE EFFECT of hiding your own line entirely; v2.6.113 turned that hard-coded test into a mode, and "Show all" then un-hid the echo as well. `chatBubbleWanted(who, src)` now knows which of the two it is looking at: an echo of your own line never paints, in any mode, and the SENDER-side bubble is the one that survives, so the bubble still appears the moment you press Enter. Johan's instinct was right — this is the same shape as the quick-phrase double we fixed at v2.6.55, arriving by a different route. **(3) The stopwatch ran on regardless.** Exactly one event had ever stopped a Reaction window — a quick phrase, via `showSay` (v2.6.33) — so a played card and a menu action both let it count down to RESOLVE while the table had plainly answered. `dfxCancel()` is that one path now, called from `showSay`; from `cardFx` on a played card or on an announced action's lock clone (verb-bearing ONLY: a bare Lock is not an answer), placed ABOVE the `animMode` early-returns so it holds for a player with animations off and fires for a remote `fx` exactly as for your own play; and locally from `act` / `actBleed` / the counter-zone card action, for the case where autoLock is off or the minion is already locked and no clone is emitted at all. **Deliberately not hooked: `tally`** — per the bot handoff the bot writes its stealth to the shared counter at EVERY announce, so a tally is no longer evidence that anybody reacted. **Residual, named:** autoLock off AND an already-locked minion leaves a remote screen with only a relayed log line, and it keeps counting; closing that needs a wire signal or log-grammar parsing, neither worth it before Johan has seen this in the browser. Gates: client 179/0, dispatch 47/0, protocol 6/0, canary 37/0, B1 byte-identical 1 277 347, check-versions green.

> **15 September 2026, latest (THE TIERS NEVER CROSS — client v2.6.114 / server v2.6.37 / bot v0.6.112, pure client — purge):** Johan asked whether a quick phrase can also trigger the new chat animation. It cannot — verified and now PINNED rather than left to be re-read: exactly one caller asks `sayBubble` for the `.chatb` variant (`chatBubble`, 13748), the quick phrase (13821) and the resolver verdict (13723) both stay loud, `showSay` never mentions `chatBubble`, and the `chat` handler never touches `showSay` or `SAY`. **And a correction I owe.** In the v2.6.113 note I repeated Johan's phrase that quick phrases have “their own setting” without checking. They have NO setting: `showSay()` goes straight to `sayBubble` with no helper, no `conv` value, not even `animMode`. The three-way he had in mind is `conv.anim` (`animMode`: on / mine / all), which governs CARD animations — the right pattern at the wrong layer. Johan's own reading is that the absence is deliberate: reach for a quick phrase and you want it seen. **Worth recording for whoever designs the missing control:** the loud tier has TWO sources, `say{i}` and the resolver verdict lines, sharing one bubble — so “hide quick phrases” and “hide verdicts” are not the same switch, and anyone adding a gate must decide which. §12.0 now carries a what-governs-each-tier table beside the what-each-tier-means one. **Deferred by Johan:** a `sayBubbles` control. The symmetric version needs a new helper key and therefore srv-2.6.38 and a restart — the same trap `chatFx` fell into against his running srv-2.6.35 — so if it is built, the local dropdown ships alone and the helper half waits for a restart that is happening anyway. Gates: client 174/0, dispatch 47/0, protocol 6/0, canary 37/0, B1 byte-identical 1309541, check-versions green.

> **15 September 2026 (WHOSE BUBBLE — client v2.6.113 / server v2.6.37 / bot v0.6.112, pure client — purge):** Johan asked for a three-way choice over the chat tier, in the Advanced fold, matching the dropdowns already there. `conv.chatBubbles`: **Hide mine** (the default), **Show all**, **Hide all**. **What made this a small change and not a fiddly one:** the “not mine” test has lived in the chat HANDLER since v2.6.50 (`if(m.who !== net.you) chatBubble(…)`), which is exactly why it could never be a setting — a policy decision sitting in a dispatcher. It moved into `chatBubbleWanted()`, one pure function the suite can drive directly, and the handler now calls `chatBubble` unconditionally. **Two gates, deliberately ordered.** `hx('chatFx')` is asked FIRST and is the table's: the host can lock the animation off and tournament mode kills it, like every helper. `conv.chatBubbles` is asked second and is yours, and it can only ever SUBTRACT — so a local preference can never show a bubble the host has locked off, and tournament mode has no hole in it. An unrecognised stored value falls back to the default rather than to “show everything”, for the same reason. The quick-phrase animation is untouched and the suite pins that `sayBubble` never consults the chat filter. Persistence, validation on load and settings-profile carriage all copy `hoverPreview`, the dropdown it sits beside. Gates: client 173/0, destructive 2/2 vs v2.6.112, dispatch 47/0, protocol 6/0, canary 37/0, B1 byte-identical 1309541, check-versions green.

> **15 September 2026 (ABSENT IS NOT OFF — client v2.6.112 / server v2.6.37 / bot v0.6.112, pure client — purge):** Johan's chat animation never fired, for his own typing or the bot's. Two findings and a move. **(1) `hx()` read ABSENT as OFF.** The server rebuilds a locked policy's `settings` map from its OWN `HELPER_KEYS` (srv 291), so `chatFx` — added to the client at v2.6.107 and to the server at srv-2.6.37 — is simply not in any policy stamped by the srv-2.6.35 Johan is running. `hx()` did `!!(p.settings && p.settings[key])`, so absent became false and `chatBubble` returned before drawing. A key the policy does not MENTION is not governed by it and falls through to the local setting now; `key in p.settings` keeps "the table says off" distinct from "the table has never heard of this", and tournament mode still overrides everything first so an unknown key cannot become a hole in it. **This was masked by a bug.** While v2.6.111's null-ing broadcast was still live, the policy kept vanishing and `hx()` fell through to the local value — so `chatFx` worked by accident, and fixing the real bug is what exposed this one. **(2) Your own chat has never bubbled, by design** (v2.6.50: `if(m.who !== net.you) chatBubble(...)`) — the bubble is receiver-side. Worth knowing before judging the tier: solo-testing with one bot, only the bot's lines should pop. **(3) The panel moved** from `bottom:64px` to `top:74px`: on a laptop viewport the pool globe covered it. Method note: a prose comment tripped a source assertion for the THIRD time this session (the new comment says "was bottom:64px"); the test now slices the cssText literal instead of matching the region. Gates: client 171/0, destructive 2/2 vs v2.6.111, dispatch 47/0, protocol 6/0, canary 37/0, B1 byte-identical 1309541, check-versions green.

> **15 September 2026 (TWO NAMES FOR ONE THING — client v2.6.111 / server v2.6.37 / bot v0.6.112, pure client — purge):** Johan created a room with **All helpers on** and sat down to only a Pass button. The console settled it in one line: `net.helperPolicy` was **null**, `hx('on')` false, `#phases` carrying `helperoff`. **The server has two names for one object.** `joined` stamps `msg.helperPolicy` (srv 411); the dedicated broadcast sends `{ t:'helperPolicy', policy }` (srv 957). The s2c handler read only `m.helperPolicy`, so the *initial* policy landed correctly and then the first CONFIRMATION of it — adding a bot (`netBotHelperLock`), a host edit, anything calling `setHelperPolicy` — nulled it on every client in the room. With the policy null, `hx()` falls to its local branch and the player's own `conv.tournament` decides: a table explicitly created as locked-all-on ran with every helper OFF, which is exactly the inversion Johan described. The handler reads `m.policy || m.helperPolicy` now, which works against **every server already deployed** — no restart, and his live srv-2.6.35 is fixed by the client alone. **Why no gate caught it, and this is the part worth keeping:** the protocol lint diffs the DOC against the CLIENT's handlers, never against what the SERVER actually sends. An s2c field-name mismatch between the two sides is invisible to the lint, the dispatch suite, the client suite and the canary alike. A prototype diff of every server send against every `m.FIELD` a handler reads flagged this correctly — and 28 false positives besides, because the server relays `say`/`fx`/`decide`/`tally` verbatim without ever naming their fields. A general gate is therefore possible but would need real relay-awareness; for now the specific contract is pinned by a test that asserts BOTH server send sites verbatim and drives the live broadcast shape through the handler. **Also fixed by the same change:** the v2.6.103 bot helper lock has been pushing a correct policy all along and watching the confirmation erase it.

> **15 September 2026 (THE MIRROR BUG — client v2.6.110 / server v2.6.37 / bot v0.6.112, pure client — purge):** Johan's first browser check, and it went red on the debug checkbox: greyed out on a non-tournament table. `botDebugAllowed()` read `conv.tournament` as a flat veto, but that is the player's PERSISTED preference, not this table's mode. The offline dropdown (`#offHelpers`) is the table's authority and was already checked; `conv.tournament` must only speak when no other authority is present. The irony: v2.6.109's comment named the first draft's bug ("a stubbed-out dropdown swallowed the conventions check") and shipped its mirror image (a conventions check that swallowed the dropdown). The harness gained an `_ids` map on the document stub so tests can inject a named element for `$()` and a `__doc` accessor so the test can reach it; the v2.6.108 debug test now covers both the with-dropdown and without-dropdown paths.

> **15 September 2026 (THE PANEL, REVIEWED — client v2.6.109 / server v2.6.37 / bot v0.6.112, pure client — purge):** Johan's standing pre-test review gate, and it earned its keep three times over on a version delivered an hour earlier. **(1) Online the panel never showed.** `hotBotBtnShow(true)` was called from the offline spawn only; every online session would have added a bot and seen nothing. `netBotAdd` mounts it after the helper lock now, and `netBotRemove` / `netBotsTeardown` ask it to collapse — which it only does when `botPanelSeats()` is empty, so removing one of three bots keeps the panel. **(2) Offline the debug row waited for a dropdown change.** `renderOfflineSeats` toggled `#offAlphaNote` when a seat became a bot but never re-synced its new sibling; it calls `botDebugSync()` now. **(3) The click race.** The 4 Hz loop did `el.innerHTML=h` every tick, and a button replaced between mousedown and mouseup loses the click — the buttons would have been intermittently unclickable in exactly the moment they matter. The rebuild is now behind a shape signature (open state, rows, names, reasons, rungs, steppability, and *whether* a deadline exists — deliberately not its value) and a plain tick rewrites only the countdown spans' text in place. **What could be tested was tested.** A new LIVE `testB` seats a real bot over hotBridge, puts it in a hold from the human seat and reads it through the panel's own `botPanelRead()`: `held` / `hold: You` / `until:null` / `release-hold` / `canStep:true`; `b.step()` returns `did:'release-hold'`, the bot then plays its turn and passes, and a second press is a no-op with a reason. The render loop was hammered 60 ticks through six `status()` shapes including throw, null and `{}` with nine open/close toggles: zero throws. **What could not be:** the harness DOM is a Proxy stub — it proves the code path does not throw, not what the panel looks like. Layout, sizes and the button feel still need a browser. Gates: client 167/0, dispatch 47/0, protocol 6/0, canary 37/0, B1 byte-identical, check-versions green. No server change.\n\n> **15 September 2026 (THE BOT PANEL — client v2.6.108 / server v2.6.37 / bot v0.6.112, pure client — purge):** the last two client items from Johan's online feedback round, both unblocked by the bot project's 15 Sep handover (R1–R4, v0.6.109–v0.6.112). **(1) The widget (#1).** Bot v0.6.109 shipped exactly the seam the 14 Sep handoff asked for: `status()` → `{state, waitingFor, until, seat, name, version, step, canStep}` and `step()`, which pulls an ALREADY EXISTING wait forward through the same method the clock would have called — so a press can never reach an outcome the bot could not have reached alone, and the bot suite stays a true statement with the button as well as without it. The panel is a collapsible robot in the corner (open state remembered in localStorage, per Johan: collapsed by default so it does not clog the UI), one row per bot: name, what it is waiting for in table language, a countdown to `until`, and a step button that labels itself from `status().step` via `BOT_STEP_LABEL`. Entirely in-process — bots live in THIS tab in both modes (`hotBots` / `netBots`) — so there is no wire change, and online it is host-only, which is correct: only the host holds the sockets. A hold shows an em-dash rather than a number because bot 615 gives it `until:null` by design — it ends when a human says so. `botPanelRead()` treats `status()` as someone else's contract: a throw, a null, a missing method or a nonsense deadline all degrade to a readable row rather than killing a 4 Hz render loop. **The offline ▶ sweep did not vanish, it moved IN** — `step()` pulls one wait forward, it does not play a turn, so the sweep is still the only manual way to run the bot seats and is now a row in the panel instead of a second floating button in the same corner. The panel joins `BOARD_CHROME`, so it swallows the wheel and never pans the table underneath — one line in the v2.6.103 constant, both gestures, which is what that constant was for. **(2) Debug bot.** A spawn option (`debug`) on both Add-bot paths, exactly as the bot project specified — the bot reads `o.debug` at construction and arms on the DEAL (`openHand` is started-gated), so this is read when a bot is added, never toggled into a live instance. They asked us to force it off in Tournament mode because the bot cannot see the table's mode; `botDebugAllowed()` refuses if ANY authority says tournament (room policy, the hotseat dropdown, or local conventions), deliberately unbranched and failing CLOSED, because refusing wrongly costs a debug aid while allowing wrongly opens a hand to the table. Bots already force helpers to `all` in both modes so this should be unreachable — but v2.6.103 found a lock that had not locked for three versions, so the guarantee is pinned by a test rather than inherited from someone else's invariant. **Protocol:** §12 records two settlements — the two block shapes (`blocks.` from us, `attempts to block.` from them) are BOTH parsed by the bot and neither is canonical, and the undirected announce family is bot-emitted only, so that doctrine fires bot-against-bot and never against a human (open, not blocking). **Checked and clean:** the bot project's v0.6.108 `Trainbot`-name regression could not reach us — every bot construction on this side passes a name (`netBotAdd`/`hotBotAdd` pass the lobby's, the suite uses `Bridgebot`/`Holdbot`/`nm`), and the only occurrence of the literal is a DECK label. **Not taken:** the four new bot knobs (`reactClockMs`, `reactPaceMs`, `politicsAsk`, `whoBlocksGraceMs`) stay at their defaults rather than becoming four number fields in the lobby before anyone knows which value actually chafes. Gates: client 164/0, destructive 5/5 vs v2.6.107, dispatch 47/0, protocol 6/0, canary 37/0, B1 byte-identical, check-versions green. No server change.

> **14 September 2026 (THREE VOICES — client v2.6.107 / server v2.6.37 / bot v0.6.108, RESTART + purge):** Johan's complaint was that a long bot chat line arrives in a font so large and so stretched across the screen that the eye cannot parse it before it fades, while the quick phrases genuinely benefit from that size. The three levels he described — status / chat / quick phrase — turned out to ALREADY EXIST on the wire as `log`, `chat` and `say{i}`; what was missing was that `chat` and `say` shared one visual recipe, so a 110-character instruction was rendered in the 58px face written for "Block!". **The fix is a variant, not a second function.** `sayBubble(role, who, seat, text, variant)` adds `chatb` to the class; `.chatb` overrides exactly three things — the typography (`clamp(15px, 1.6vw, 24px)` against the phrase face's `clamp(22px, 4.5vw, 58px)`), the measure (`min(46ch, 70vw)`, the classic readable line length, so text WRAPS in an invisible column instead of stretching) and the medallion (190px behind a 16px line would swallow it, scaled to 110px). One mounting path and one shared 3-bubble eviction budget, so a parallel `chatBubble()` mount can never drift from it. Centred was Johan's call over left-aligned, to be judged in the browser. **A new helper is not a pure client change.** `HELPER_DEFS` has a hand-written mirror in the server's `HELPER_KEYS` (srv 286), and `cleanHelperPolicy` builds its settings map from that list ALONE — so `chatFx` in the client without `chatFx` in the server would be silently dropped from every host-locked policy. Hence srv-2.6.37 and a restart. A client test now diffs the two lists in both directions so the pair cannot drift again. The chat clamp went 120 → 180 characters (a readable column fits more), and the gate sits in `chatBubble`, never in the `chat` handler: turning the animation off must not cost you the message. **Protocol:** new §12.0 writes down what the three channels MEAN — status / speech / a call — with the announce-then-ask double-bubble named as the known offender. That is the handoff for the bot project's half (#7b): move the card-play announce from `chat` to `log`. **Parked (Johan, 14 Sep):** votes can be neutral, and the Vote submenu could feed the Played window's own For/Against counters so neutral votes can be bumped by hand. Additive — the minion's `casts N votes for.` line and the resolver's `Vote: A for vs B against — vote passes.` line are separately frozen, so wiring the menu into the counter breaks neither. Gates: client 159/0, destructive 4/4 vs v2.6.106, dispatch 47/0, protocol 6/0, canary 37/0, B1 byte-identical 1309541, check-versions green.

> **14 September 2026 (THE CONTRACT ROUND — client v2.6.106 / server v2.6.36 / bot v0.6.108, pure client — purge):** the handoff round: everything the sister project needs from us for politics and for blocking actions a bot cannot recognise, shipped as contract BEFORE either side writes behaviour. **(1) `SAY` +2, appended.** 8 `Any blocks?`, 9 `Any reaction?` — the asking side of two phrases that until now existed only as answers, so a human can prompt a bot the way a bot already prompts a human. Appended and never reordered, because `say` carries an INDEX on the wire and the bot mirrors the array (bot 390): an insertion anywhere else renumbers every phrase for every peer on an older build, silently, with no error at either end. **(2) A Vote submenu under Actions.** `Political action` goes through `act()` — it locks, closes the play batch and carries the target suffix — so a bot's block step reaches it on exactly the path a bleed takes, which is the whole point: the bot has no politics model, but it can still be asked to block. `Cast votes → For… / Against…` prompts for a count (clamped 1–99 like Bleed X) and announces WITHOUT locking or closing the batch, because a titled minion keeps its votes while locked. **(3) §12 freezes what the client actually says.** The bot's own grammar table has carried the human client's wording as *"unverified (main-project ask)"* since THE SPLIT — `blocks.`, `hunts.`, `attempts to rescue a vampire from torpor.`, `commits diablerie!` (the one action line with no period), `takes a card action.`, `NAME's card action is used.` for a non-minion counter-zone card, plus the two new vote shapes with their singular/plural rule. New §12.1 freezes the `say{i}` index table itself and states the append-only rule and the tolerate-unknown-index rule that make a mixed-version room safe. A test parses §12.1's table out of the doc and compares it row for row against the live `SAY`, so the two cannot drift. What this unblocks in the bot project: the `Any blocks?` / `Any reaction?` reaction hook, the "Will this action cause me to lose pool?" politics question with its pool bookkeeping held until Resolved, and the block doctrine's rule that a human who right-clicks Block before reaching for the quick phrase has ALREADY announced the block. Gates: client 156/0, destructive 3/3 vs v2.6.105, dispatch 47/0, protocol 6/0, canary 37/0, B1 byte-identical, check-versions green. No server change.

> **14 September 2026 (CLOSE TABLE — client v2.6.105 / server v2.6.36 / bot v0.6.108, RESTART + purge):** Johan's report was that a room stayed in the list after he left as host "because the bot was still there", and the answer turned out to be three separate things wearing one coat. **(1) Closing had no verb.** `kick` exists but takes one player at a time; looping it from the client would have been a sequence of side effects rather than an intention, and the last kick would have left the host alone in a room the emptiness rule then deleted. New REF_VERB `closeRoom` → `closeRoomNow(room, msg)`: broadcast `closed{msg}` FIRST so every client knows why, delete the room, then close every socket; idempotent (`rooms.has` guard) because two paths can now race to close the same room. **(2) The server could not tell a human from a bot.** `p.bot` has existed since 13 July but only ever fed the roster's control-my-elements exception — nothing ever counted it. `humansLeft(room)` is that census, and it deliberately counts ROSTER SEATS, not sockets: a human who drops their connection is still a player and keeps their room (that is what the reconnect window is for), while a human who really leaves is gone. A real `leave` that takes the last non-bot player now closes the room immediately instead of letting an unplayable table sit in the list. **(3) The 60-minute rule was written for a real match.** A room holding bots with no human ONLINE gets `BOT_ROOM_GRACE_MS` (10 min) instead — online bots live in the host's tab, so once the human is gone so are they. That also bounds the one case v2.6.104 could not reach: a host who closes the tab sends no helper-policy restore, and now the locked policy dies with the room in ten minutes rather than outliving the session by an hour. **Client:** `Host…` gains `Close table…` (online only — a hotseat has no room to close), and `Leave seat` asks a host with company which they mean via the existing `uiDialog` three-way: Leave only / Close the table / Cancel, with cancel doing neither. `mpCanCloseRoom()` is the pure gate and bots do NOT count as company, because with the new server rule a host alone with bots gets the same outcome either way — asking would be a question with one real answer. The `closed` handler sets `net.status='off'` BEFORE calling `mpLeave`, which both skips the pointless `leave` send and stops `ws.onclose` from starting its 2.5 s rejoin loop against a room the server has already deleted. Gates: client 153/0, destructive vs v2.6.104, dispatch 47/0, destructive 4/4 vs srv-2.6.35, protocol 6/0, canary 37/0, B1 byte-identical, check-versions green. **Deploy: server restart + Cloudflare purge.**

> **14 September 2026 (THE POLICY GOES BACK BEFORE THE DOOR — client v2.6.104 / server v2.6.35 / bot v0.6.108, pure client — purge):** Johan's "anything odd?" gate, one delivery later, and the answer was yes. v2.6.103 made the bot helper lock a real lock (`allowLocal:false`) — and that promoted a dormant asymmetry into a live bug: removing the last bot restores the policy, but LEAVING the room did not, so a host who left with bots still seated handed the remaining players a helper-locked table with nothing left holding the lock. Before v2.6.103 it did not matter, because the 'lock' it left behind was the ordinary `allowLocal:true` default. The obvious home for the restore, `netBotsTeardown`, is the WRONG one: `mpLeave` sends `leave` at 8334 and the server runs `dropPlayer(conn, true)` (srv 1261), clearing `conn.room`/`conn.player` — so by the teardown call at 8340 every GAME_HANDLER answers 'Join a room first' and the push would bounce silently. `mpLeave` now calls `netBotHelperUnlock(true)` BEFORE the leave verb; the unlock gained a quiet flag so a teardown does not toast, and the teardown's comment states why the restore is deliberately absent there. Two tests pin the ORDER on the wire, not merely the presence. Residual and named: a host who closes the tab sends nothing at all, so the room keeps the locked policy — the same root as the room-survives-a-closed-tab report, and the durable answer is server-side in the Close table round. Gates: client 150/0, destructive 2/2 vs v2.6.103, dispatch 44/0, protocol 6/0, canary 37/0, B1 byte-identical, check-versions green. No server change.

> **14 September 2026 (ONE RULE FOR THE FELT — client v2.6.103 / server v2.6.35 / bot v0.6.108, pure client — purge):** two finds from Johan's first online bot sessions, both root-caused deeper than they looked. **(1) The wheel zoomed the board through the pool globe.** The reported symptom was "scrolling to change pool also zooms", and the obvious fix — a missing `stopPropagation` on `#pool` and `#dockPool`, the only two of six wheel consumers inside `#board` that lacked it — would have been a patch on the symptom. The real cause is that `#board` carries TWO gesture handlers with different ideas of what bare felt is: the pan handler (12260) has always had an exclusion list, the wheel handler (12249) had none, so whether a wheel reached the zoom depended entirely on whether the child under the pointer happened to stop it. Both now read ONE constant, `BOARD_CHROME`, through `isBoardChrome()`; the pan handler's hand-written copy of the list is gone. Johan's rule: chrome and cards swallow the wheel — with or without an effect of their own — and open board zooms. Surfaces are deliberately absent from the list (`#z-ready`, `#z-uncontrolled`, `#z-torpor`, the opponents' mats): in L3 they are most of the screen, and blocking them would leave nowhere to zoom. Two accepted consequences, both agreed with Johan first: cards in the crypt/library/ash piles and the hand fan stop zooming (ready/uncontrolled/torpor cards already swallowed the wheel for blood counters, so this makes the last cards consistent), and a drag on the log, the zoom slider, a pile or the hold badge no longer starts an L4 pan. The two missing `stopPropagation` calls are fixed too, as belt-and-braces rather than as the mechanism, and `#phases` + the resolver counters follow the same rule though they sit outside `#board` and never reached the zoom. **(2) The bot helper lock had never locked anything.** `netBotHelperLock` (v2.6.99) hand-built `{settings: all true, allowLocal: true, tournament: false}` — but `allowLocal:true` IS the 'local' mode: `hx()` (7543) falls straight through to each player's own Settings when it is set, and the server's `helperMode()` (srv 248) reads the room back as 'local', so the room list did not even tag it. Every helper was switched on in a field nobody reads. It calls `helperPolicyFor('all')` now — the create path's own function, one source for the shape — which makes `allowLocal:false` and lights the existing lobby banner ("Helpers locked to the host's settings"), so the lock is visible instead of silent; a toast names it on lock and on restore. Johan's alternative (a create-time requirement, like the Structured gate) was deliberately NOT taken: `boardMode` needs a create gate because no verb changes it, while `setHelperPolicy` is a lobby verb, so a create-time requirement would lock out rooms that could simply be corrected on the spot. **Also:** the suite's hotseat-hold end-to-end test pinned a bot gap as a known failure ("a hold placed while no bot is relevant is never announced"); bot **v0.6.108** closed it, so the pin was stale in the good direction and now asserts the announcement. PROJECT-MANIFEST §E2 was two releases behind (v0.6.102) and reads v0.6.108. Gates: client 148/0, destructive 4/4 vs v2.6.102, dispatch 44/0, protocol 6/0, canary 37/0, endpoint 21/1 (the cardfx static, no `elysium-cardfx.json` beside the server here), B1 byte-identical 1 247 018, check-versions green. No server change.

> **13 September 2026 (THE TABLE WAS NEVER FULL — client v2.6.102 / server v2.6.35 / bot v0.6.107, pure client — purge):** Johan's first browser-pass find, and a plain bug of mine: Add bot refused with "The table is full" for a host sitting alone. `netBotWhy` compared the player count against the lobby snapshot's `seats` — which is NOT a capacity. The server sets `seats: room.players.length` (srv 318) as the seat-picker's RANGE, so a lone host read 1 >= 1 and the gate closed. The real ceiling is `MAX_PLAYERS` (srv 56), enforced at join as `room.players.length >= MAX_PLAYERS` (srv 1072) with no filtering. The client now caps on `MP_MAX_PLAYERS`, a lockstep mirror, checked exactly as the server checks it. Walked 1→7 players: allowed through 5, refused at 6 with the server's own wording. Gates: client 145/0, destructive 1/1, canary 37/0, dispatch 44/0, protocol 6/0, B1 byte-identical 1 243 921, check-versions green.

> **13 September 2026 (THE HOLD BADGE FOLLOWS THE TABLE — client v2.6.101 / server v2.6.35 / bot v0.6.107, pure client — purge):** The hold badge was gated on `localTable`, so the ONLINE bot tables added in v2.6.99 had no visible hold state at all — a forgotten "Hold on…" froze them with nothing on screen, the exact silence v2.6.96 closed offline. A hole introduced by omission, caught in Johan's post-delivery pass. The badge now follows any table that HAS bots: bridge seats offline, the server's own roster `bot` flag online (so it is true for every player, not only the host who added them). Online the name-keying solves itself — every human has their own client and speaks in their own name — so the rule is: you release your OWN hold with a real `It resolves` broadcast (a badge that only cleared local state would leave the bots held), and another player's hold is shown WITHOUT a Resume button, so a frozen table explains itself instead of offering a control that cannot work. Inbound `say` now feeds the badge, and leaving an online room clears it as `leaveHotseat` does offline. One deliberate tightening: tracking now requires the table to have bots, not merely to be local — a hold with nothing to hold is not state worth keeping. Two v2.6.96 tests were updated to that semantics. Gates: client 145/0, destructive 4/4, canary 37/0, dispatch 44/0, protocol 6/0, B1 byte-identical 1 243 412, check-versions green.

> **13 September 2026 (THE POLICY SNAPSHOT READS THE ROOM — client v2.6.100 / server v2.6.35 / bot v0.6.107, pure client — purge):** Caught in Johan's post-delivery 'anything unsure?' pass. `netBotHelperLock` remembered the helper policy by reading the Settings host-box checkboxes — which hold their HTML defaults if the host never opened that dialog — so removing the last bot could restore a policy the room never had. It now snapshots `net.helperPolicy`, the server-sent live policy (set at 8362 on join and 8581 on change), with the old read kept only as a fallback for the should-not-happen case of no policy pushed yet. Also verified while checking: `bot.close()` sets `_closing` and clears the reconnect timers, so a kicked bot with `reconnect:true` does not rejoin. Gates: client 141/0, destructive 1/1, canary 37/0, dispatch 44/0, protocol 6/0, B1 byte-identical 1 241 263, check-versions green.

> **13 September 2026 (ONLINE BOTS FROM THE LOBBY — client v2.6.99 / server v2.6.35 / bot v0.6.107, pure client — purge):** Johan's ask, so he can compare bot play online against the hotseat he tested offline. The host adds bots from the LOBBY, reactively, so a dropped or no-show player can be replaced mid-lobby. Each bot is a second browser WebSocket to the same server (`netBotDial` reads `net.addr`/`net.sec`), joining `net.room` with `net.pass` — entirely separate from `net.ws`. **No bridge is involved:** online the server IS the bus, so the hold, the referee verbs, board pubs and the fan-out are all native. That is what makes this the fidelity test for the bridge — a table that plays differently offline means the bridge differs, not the bot. Host-only, Full mode, Structured rooms only: `boardMode` is fixed at room creation (srv 1327) and no verb changes it, so a Classic room shows the reason on a disabled button rather than spawning a bot that will misplay. Helpers CAN change in the lobby (`setHelperPolicy` is a REF_VERB), so the first bot locks the policy to every helper on + no tournament mode, mirroring v2.6.93 offline, and removing the last bot restores what was there before. Seats are set on the bot's own socket with the same `seat` verb a human uses. Kicking a bot closes its socket too, or it would simply rejoin. Bots live in the host's tab and are torn down on leave. Same decks, personas, `paceMs 3100` and `askSecs 20` as offline, so the comparison is like-for-like. **Proved live against a real server** (localhost, real bot v0.6.107): two bots joined, took seats via the `seat` verb, the host's `start` started the game, and each bot's `this.table` read [1, 3] and [1, 2] — which also settles, by running it, the one claim the 12 Sep handoff had to mark as read-not-run. Also worth noting for the LOCAL server: until now there was no way at all to seat a bot there without running the bot's CLI by hand, one process per bot. Gates: client 140/0, destructive 5/5, canary 37/0, dispatch 44/0, protocol 6/0, B1 byte-identical 1 241 213, check-versions green. No server change.

> **12 September 2026 (THE BOTS CAN SEE THE TABLE — client v2.6.98 / server v2.6.35 / bot v0.6.107, pure client — purge):** Johan's call, and the right one: blind bots make a test measure their card play rather than their decision logic, so you cannot tell what you are looking at. Measured before the change: after a full 2-bot hotseat game every bot's `this.table` was EMPTY — no seat at all. Online the server broadcasts every board push (srv line 1134) and hands a joiner every existing pub (398/1412), which is the input behind `_tableThreat` (who is the table threat), `_rushCandidates` (what there is to rush) and `_checkAshurTableWide` (Johan's July ask: react to what a HUMAN plays). Offline none of it had a source — `schedulePush`/`pushNow` return early when not `inRoom()`, and v2.6.95's bus carried log/say/chat/fx but deliberately not board. v2.6.98 puts board on the bus: each bot's pub fans out to the other bots, and the active human's board is pushed to every bot seat on the same 600 ms debounce as online, seeded at `startHotseat` the way the server seeds a joiner. Measured after: Bot1 sees seats [1, 3], Bot2 sees [1, 2], with real pools and card counts, and `_tableThreat` returns a real score instead of running on nothing. Gates: client 135/0 no skips, destructive 3/3, canary 37/0, dispatch 44/0, protocol 6/0, B1 byte-identical 1 230 537, check-versions green.

> **12 September 2026 (THE REFEREE VERBS REACH THE BOT — client v2.6.97 / server v2.6.35 / bot v0.6.107, pure client — purge):** Johan asked whether his long-standing rule — any player may control a bot seat's elements, since a headless bot can't model every triggered ability (v0.4.4, 13 July) — actually holds today. It held for CARDS: blood ±1, lock, flip, blue/green counters and torpor all ride `hotCtrl` on a bot seat, gated on the roster `bot` flag that `startHotseat` sets correctly. It did NOT hold for the seat-level referee verbs. `hostSetPoolHot` edited the stored pub while the bot kept its own `this.pool`, so the bot overwrote the edit on its next publish — measured against bot v0.6.107: pool set to 5, bot still holding 30, display back to 28 the moment it acted. `hostOustHot` likewise marked the roster while the bot played on. Both receivers (`forceSetPool`, `forceOust`) already existed on the bot; only the sender was missing, exactly as with the log fan-out. v2.6.97 adds `hotBotSeat(seat)` (roster bot AND connected on the bridge AND a local table) and routes both verbs through it. A drain to zero goes through the full oust path instead of `forceSetPool(0)`, because the bot's own clamp-and-oust would otherwise skip the predator's bounty and the roster flag and leave half a state. The quick Pool ± steps — built for political actions, which is exactly when a bot miscounts — are no longer online-only. Gates: client 132/0 no skips, destructive 4/4, canary 37/0, dispatch 44/0, protocol 6/0, B1 byte-identical 1 228 304, check-versions green.

> **12 September 2026 (THE HOTSEAT BUS + THE VISIBLE HOLD — client v2.6.96 / server v2.6.35 / bot v0.6.107, pure client — purge):** Johan uploaded bot v0.6.107 and asked what the CLIENT could do to make human-vs-bot play land well (he tests 1 human + 2 bots, then 2 + 2). Measuring against the real bot found the hotseat bridge was a STAR with no fan-out: the human's public log lines reached no bot (so a bleed never registered — the bot's L12 grammar reads those frozen lines), and two bots were mutually deaf, so v0.6.107's headline undirected reaction window could never fire. The fallback channel was shut too: `#chatRow` is hidden while `net.status==='off'`, so the interface the bots advertise in their opening line ("bleed N", "@Name bleed N") had no input offline. **v2.6.95** adds `hotFanOut()` — the human's lines ride `mpRelay` to every bot, each bot's log/say/chat/fx fans out to the OTHER bots, always excluding the sender. Loop safety is free from `log()`'s own contract: bot-injected lines are logged `localOnly=true` and never reach `mpRelay`. The chat line now exists offline and speaks onto the bus. ONE exception, found by soaking a 2-bot game to a deadlock in round 3: a BOT's "Hold on…" is not relayed to other bots — the bot filters `players[].bot` in `_holdHook` but NOT in the ask-window pause (`a.pausedBy`), so a bot could park another bot's undirected window with no timer and nothing to release it. Reported for the bot project; the bus filters it meanwhile. **v2.6.96** makes the hold visible and releasable: a badge names who holds and one click resumes, relaying `It resolves` PER HOLDER NAME (measured: Bob's ordinary release left the bots' holder set reading ["Alice"] forever, and `_pace()` + `hotRunBotTurns` both wait unbounded, so a forgotten hold froze the table with nothing on screen). Say bubbles now live 3 s for a quick phrase and up to 9 s for a long line; PREY/PREDATOR labels work at a hotseat. Gates: client 128/0 with NO skips (the bot file is present — canary 37/0), destructive 9/9, dispatch 44/0, protocol 6/0, B1 byte-identical 1 226 382, check-versions green.

> **12 September 2026 (THE SAY BUBBLE CLAMP — client v2.6.94 / server v2.6.35 / bot v0.6.102, pure client — purge):** Johan's report: a bot's initial chat message spilled outside the board on a laptop because `.sayb` had `white-space:nowrap` and `.saytxt` was a fixed 58px Cinzel. Now: `.sayb` gets `max-width:min(92vw,92%)` + `overflow-wrap:break-word` (no more nowrap); `.saytxt` uses `clamp(22px, 4.5vw, 58px)` and `.saywho` uses `clamp(12px, 1.4vw, 18px)` — the font scales with the viewport, so short quick-phrases ('Block!', 'Pass') stay big on desktop while a 120-char chat line wraps and shrinks on a laptop or phone. `chatBubble`'s 120-char JS guard stays. Gates: client 115/0 (+2; destructive vs v2.6.90), dispatch 44/0, protocol lint 6/0, B1 byte-identical 1 217 279, check-versions green.

> **12 September 2026 (THE TOURNAMENT OVERRIDE IS A SESSION — client v2.6.92 / server v2.6.35 / bot v0.6.102, pure client — purge):** Johan's 'anything else odd?' gate caught it via the new header: a hotseat's Helpers choice (`startOfflineFromCfg`) and the tutorial's Classic rule wrote `conv.tournament` straight for the session, and `leaveHotseat` never wrote it back -- the v2.6.51 'session-only, no saveConv' held only until the NEXT `saveConv()` (any Settings change, a Lite|Full switch, dismissing the welcome bubble), which persisted the table's value as the player's preference; the Tournament checkbox flipped silently and the v2.6.91 header read '(custom)' after a Structured hotseat. Now `hotTournSession(val)` snapshots the player's own value once (`hotTournBefore`) and overrides; `leaveHotseat` calls `hotTournRestore()` beside the `helperAllOn=false` it always did; a deliberate edit of the Settings checkbox DURING a table updates the snapshot so the restore does not undo it; the resumed Classic tutorial (`tutGameResume`) applies the rule too -- it relied on the leak before. The only bare writes left are the loader, the import, the checkbox handler and the two inside the pair (the suite counts them: 5). > **11 September 2026 (THE SETTINGS HEADER + THE 1v1 FIND — client v2.6.91 / server v2.6.35 / bot v0.6.102, pure client — purge):** Johan's two finds; bot-vs-bot parked at his word (the analysis: a hotseat spectator identity, a continuous turn loop with pause/stop, and the pre-existing gaps that an all-bot table makes acute -- bots never self-oust at a hotseat and the sweep does not skip `out` seats). **(1) The Settings header** is a flex row: SETTINGS · 'Lite mode' / 'Full mode', '(custom)' the moment anything in the dialog differs from that mode's defaults · a **Reset** on the right. The PROFILE (`settingsProfile` / `settingsDefaults(mode)` / `settingsDiff`, all pure and in the suite) is everything the dialog edits and nothing else: 29 conv keys (`SETTINGS_CONV_KEYS`), the per-channel sound levels as EFFECTIVE values (`{}` and the literal `{unlock:150,…}` are the same profile), every `HELPER_DEFS` flag, every `ACTIONS` binding, both brightness sliders, the autosave cadence; the two modes' defaults differ only in `UI_PRESET`'s trio. Outside it: `uiMode`, the other mode's `uiProf` memory, `modeHintDone`, `tutDone`, `dbDefault`, the host's policy box and all DATA -- this is not the factory reset under Files & storage. Reset is LIVE (`settingsApplyProfile` mirrors every control's change handler: saveConv/saveHelper/saveKeys + the three localStorage keys, then animMode, applyPco, refreshPoolColor, applyTouchUI, applyHelper/syncHelperUI, rebuildKeymap, applyBrightness/applyUiBrightness, applyAutosaveMode, the edge token, loadCardDB if the database was off; then `openSettings()`), asks first, and is refused with a reason during a live table/lobby (`settingsResetWhy` -- a hotseat overrides conv.tournament for the session) or the tutorial; it is greyed when nothing differs. '(custom)' repaints through a delegated input/change/click listener on `#settingsModal` (each control's own handler runs first) plus a hook in `saveKeys()` (a rebinding lands via a document keydown). **(2) The 1v1 find:** the offline deck dropdown wrote `offlineCfg[i].deckIdx` but never re-synced Start -- 'toggle a bot, then pick your deck' left the button disabled and the hint 'Pick a deck for: You' stale until something re-rendered the seats (a second bot did). Every seat-row listener (name, deck, bot deck) now goes through `offlineSeatSet(i,key,val)` → `offlineSyncStart()` (which returns the readiness). The all-bot refusal in `offlineReady` stays. Gates: client 109/0 (+5; destructive 5/5 vs v2.6.90), dispatch 44/0, protocol lint 6/0, endpoint smoke 20/0 (+2 bot statics fail without the bot files beside -- the server is untouched), canary skipped without the bot, B1 byte-identical 1 214 195, check-versions green. Open: `importSettings` runs only half the apply chain (pre-existing) -- it can reuse `settingsApplyProfile`.

> **11 September 2026 (PER-ROOM REFEREE — client v2.6.83 / server v2.6.35 / bot v0.6.102, restart + purge):** Johan's clarification turned into the better model: the referee password belongs to the ROOM, set by the host at creation. **Server:** `create{refPass}` → `room.refHash`; `join{refPass}` → verified against the room (wrong key = err + the room-password strike; a room without one errs 'This room has no referee password') → the joiner is a watcher with `conn.ref=true`, `joined{ref:true}`, `watchers[]/watcherList[].ref`; the watcher dispatch routes `REF_VERBS` (the 15 host-gated verbs) to `GAME_HANDLERS` with a pseudo-p `{name:'⚖ '+wname, token:null, ref:true}`; every gate is `hostOrRef(conn, room, p)` (host token, operator `conn.admin`, or this room's referee — scoped to `conn.room===room`); `roomList.rooms[].ref` (the srv-2.6.34 server-level flag is gone, never deployed); `dropWatcher` clears `conn.ref`. The operator key (`--referee-pass`/`--admin-pass`, hello `admin`) stays for `--create-policy admin` and passes every gate. **Client:** Create game → Game settings gains 'Referee password' (`#mpRefRoom`, Full) → `refPass` on create; Join as referee sends `refPass` on the join itself (no hello, no key on the list request); `joined.ref` → `net.isRef`, and the client's host gates read `net.isHost||net.isRef` (seat menu moderation, the Host… menu, lobby Start + kick/promote/mute, 💾 matches, helper-policy push, the seating-panel spectator moderation); ⚖ marks referee'd rooms in the list and referees in the lobby/seating spectator lists; the Local server field is 'Server referee key' (operator only). `mpRefSync` no longer greys anything. **Tutorial:** oh-admin dropped, `oh-referee` (#mpRefRoom, after oh-tournlock) added; oj-spect and os-dialog rewritten. Dispatch 44/0 (the per-room test replaces the srv-2.6.34 one; destructive 1/1 vs srv-2.6.34), client 101/0 (+4; destructive 2/2 vs v2.6.82), protocol lint 6/0, smoke 20/0 (+2 bot statics), B1 byte-identical 1 204 907, check-versions green. Browser check: create with a referee password → ⚖ in the list → Join as referee → the Host menu and Start as a spectator; a plain spectator still chat-only.

Also: Join as referee with an empty key toasts 'Enter the referee password' instead of silently joining as a plain spectator, and the status line says (referee). a Lackey-comparable entry). **Lite/Full interface mode, one file:** `conv.uiMode` ('full' | 'lite') → `document.body.classList` 'lite' applied right after loadConv (before the welcome's first paint) and by `applyUiMode()`; JS gates `uiLite()` behind them (openDeckLab/openDeckLabBeside/openOffline toast 'available in Full mode'). **Lite start screen:** Settings / Create game / Join game + the `#modeToggle` pill (Lite | Full) top-right of the card and `#modeHint`, a one-time first-visit bubble (`conv.modeHintDone`, dismissed by Got it, the pill, or any start-screen action). **Menu in Lite:** Play… → Create game / Join game / Offline (disabled, tooltip) / Tutorial; Decks… → Load deck / Deck lab (disabled, tooltip); a new row 'Switch to Lite/Full mode' (disabled while `localTable || inRoom() || net.lobby`). `renderItems` gained `it.title`; `.mi.disabled` keeps pointer events (tooltips). **The dialog:** `openMP(flavor)` → `mpSetFlavor('create'|'join'|'')` sets `#mpModal[data-mp]` + the title; classes `.mpFull` (Server address, Server/Admin password, Board style, Helpers, Tournament lock, anon, the long note), `.mpCreateOnly .mpGs` (Match length, Reaction timer, chat, spectator chat — folded behind `#mpGsToggle` until `.gsOpen`), `.mpJoinOnly` (Join as spectator, List rooms, #mpRooms, the Join button); > **8 September 2026 (SECOND HARVEST — client v2.6.64 / server v2.6.30, pure client, purge only; live is STILL v2.6.61 — nothing since 11 Aug has been deployed):** two late-Aug/Sep digests. (a) Chrome's `reading 'x'` ×2 = the v2.6.63 pinch bug in V8 phrasing (dist() row then mid() row = one cycle) — confirms, adds nothing. (b) `t.entries.at`/`this.i.at is not a function` ×2 = minified names, ZERO matches in our source → Cloudflare's same-origin injected scripts (`/cdn-cgi/`) crashing in a browser without `Array.prototype.at` (Safari <15.4 / Chrome <92). Real finding regardless: our client reads zones with `.at(-1)` in 17 places, so that player would crash on their first draw. **Shipped:** (1) `_atPolyfill` on Array+String when missing (the ONLY API-level gap — census: no structuredClone/randomUUID/replaceAll/hasOwn/findLast). (2) **Legacy guard:** an ES5 `<script id="legacyGuard">` in head waits 6 s for `window.__elysiumBooted` (the main script's LAST statement); unset = never parsed (syntax cannot be shimmed) or threw at top level → a plain notice naming the browser floor instead of a dead page; the main script removes a fired notice if it boots late. **The id on that tag is load-bearing:** split_client.py, elysium-build.js and the harness all key on the ONE attribute-less script tag — and the harness regex even matched the tag spelled out inside a comment (learnings). (3) **Crash notice** (Johan: "say something when we know something broke"): ONE 9 s toast per session on the first crash ping — skips `Script error.` and `/cdn-cgi/` sources, honest about whether a report went out (errPost's return), reload hint, pointer to Report a problem so crash pings funnel into full B-63 reports; `toast(msg, ms)` gained an optional duration. Harness: window Proxy now PERSISTS writes (was: discarded) so the boot flag reads back. Suite 76→79/0 (destructive 3 vs delivered v2.6.63), dispatch 42/0, canary 37/0, bot 721/0, B1 byte-identical, check-versions green. Offered, not built: a digest filter that folds `/cdn-cgi/` rows into one "Cloudflare-script noise ×N" line.

> **19 August 2026 (FIRST LIVE TELEMETRY HARVEST — client v2.6.63 / server v2.6.30, pure client, purge only):** the B-54/B-63 pipeline paid for itself: 18 crash rows, three browser families, zero user reports needed. **Pinch crash (10 of 14, WebKit/iPad), root-caused end to end:** cancelSingles()'s synthetic pointercancel events (the v2.5.39 learning, dispatched FOR the felt-pan mirror + pile-drag cleanups) were also heard by the pinch tracker's OWN document-capture end(), emptying `pts` at the exact moment of activation — d0=dist() read a[0].x of an EMPTY array (×5), pinch stayed true over an empty map, and the next single-finger move read a[1].x of a ONE-entry array (×5) before finger-up healed it: a perfect alternating 1:1 cycle, latent since v2.5.41 because it only fires in L3 on touch (Pixel testing ran duel view). Fix: `_synth=1` on the synthetic events + end() ignores them + three belt-and-braces guards (self-heal, clean bail, <2-point refusal). **escapeHtml null-hardened** (the Firefox "s is null" row — sink fix beats caller-guessing). Clusters B/C (faceDown ×1, tx ×2) await the raw JSONL rows: src/line/col/stack are on disk and map to v2.6.61 line numbers. 'Script error.' ×4 = cross-origin/extension noise, no detail by design. Gates: client 76/0, canary 37/0, B1 byte-identical 1309541, check-versions green.

> **11 August 2026 (B-63 DIAGNOSTIC DUMP — client v2.6.60 / server v2.6.29 / digest v1.3, server restart + Cloudflare purge; v2.6.59/srv-2.6.28 deliberately SKIPPED — in flight on the parallel bot track, the merge renumbers per the 27 July precedent):** every "Report a problem" now carries `dump:{game,env}` — the FULL player-visible table (own PUBZ cards + every seat's published pub cards; an opponent's face-down card is a position-only stub WITHOUT a name — the filter is CLIENT-side at capture in `_dumpCard`/`buildDumpBoard` (T4), which matters most in hotseat where buildPub shares names between local seats; the server never invents an `n` but cannot verify a modified client) + the reporter's OWN hand names (D2 — theirs to share, the consent text says so) + vitals: ws health (`net.reconnects`/`lastMsgAt` new), card-DB tier (`cardDBSrc` new, stamped in processCardData), storage.estimate (T3 typeof-guarded), saved-deck census with legality warns (B-53 poisoning visible in every report), the four non-sensitive flags. `errRing` 40→200 (`RING_MAX`), entries ride as `{t,m}`; crash pings stay last-10-lightweight. `fitReport()` degrade ladder (ring 200→120→80 → board detail stripped → dump dropped with `dumpTrunc:1`) guarantees the report NEVER fails for being fat; a buildDump throw sends `dumpErr:1` (T7). > **8 August 2026 (HB2a — THE CONTRACT + THE ARCHITECTURE; pure analysis, no code changed):** Johan's real-browser HB1 run came back green (both serving modes). The standing-question discipline then bit MY OWN enumeration: yesterday's "18 verbs" was wrong — `_drain` transforms a queued `{__board:true}` sentinel into `{t:'board', pub: buildPub()}` at flush (push() enqueues, _pushQ dedupes), so the board verb never exists as a literal and both greps missed it. Architecturally decisive: **the bot IS board-visible** — the hotseat bridge mirrors nothing; pubs arrive like a remote player's. Definitive contract, choke-point-enumerated + runtime-verified: **bot→bridge 19 verbs**, **bridge→bot 24 types** (Object.keys(b._handlers); the regex route had both false positives — library/crypt were deck-upload fields — and misses — fx, forceOust). Server facts: fx = sanitizing broadcast relay; board c2s stores p.pub, relays, feeds the resource ledger. Hotseat read: active seat live, others JSON blobs (mutators parse→poke→re-stringify), net.boards[seat].pub is the rendered mat. **elysium-hotseat-bots-plan.md v1.0 written**: bot seat = a third seat kind, a REMOTE PLAYER with NO blob — pubs via the existing mat path, actions as §12 lines in the shared log, client's structured lines routed into the bot's parsers, and mutators ROUTE wire verbs (forceSetPool/tally/forceOust/bounty) instead of poking blobs. Slicing HB2b–e (harness-testable bridge core → 🤖 UI → full loop → visibility polish); three design questions posed to Johan (announce discipline, throttling pause, seat model); known risks incl. the v1 save/load limitation (a loaded game re-seats bots fresh) and the serialize/undo audit HB2b owns. > **7 August 2026 (THE LEGAL-ONLY SWITCHES — client v2.6.59 / server v2.6.27, client-only, no restart):** Johan's follow-up to the ghost-card round: rename the deck gates so nobody mixes them up with Tournament mode. The survey proved him righter than the question sounded — the Settings hint's ENTIRE self-description is "Tournament mode — turn all helpers off"; the deck gates v2.6.55/57 hung on the same toggle are mentioned nowhere, so the name already lied. The fix is a SPLIT, not a rename: two new switches under their own "Deck import" section — **Legal deck only** (size, 12+/60–90) and **Legal cards only** (names the card DB knows) — and Tournament mode returns to being exactly what its hint always claimed: helpers. A Tournament TABLE in the hotseat dropdown still requires both BY DEFINITION; online tournLock untouched. The core is the MIGRATION: loadConv seeds both switches from the saved tournament value when the fields are absent (the fx/pcoHome pattern in the same function), so nobody's effective behavior changes at the upgrade; fresh installs default true/true (= the old tournament:true riding). All refusal dialogs re-worded to teach the switch that actually caused the stop (with a rider when a Tournament table gates on its own); Deck Lab meter "tournament-legal sizes" → "legal deck sizes". Suite catch worth keeping: the harness conv is MUTATED by earlier tests (hx tests leave tournament=false) — defaults are asserted off a fresh loadClient. Johan's simultaneous code-review ask yielded three honest finds: deckLabel's dropdown ⚠ is size-only (a misspelled SAVED deck shows clean until loaded — cheap candidate: fold deckUnknownNames into _bad; parked pending kör); the migration lines are not suite-testable (loadConv is inline module-load code) — read-verified, honestly flagged; policy-tournament rooms never enforced deck gates (impOk always read LOCAL conv; only tournLock enforces, size-only, server-side) — pre-existing semantics, unchanged, now clearly named. Gates: client 67→68/0, dispatch 39/0, protocol 6/0, canary 37/0, bot 721/0 alone, B1 byte-identical 1 140 696, check-versions green.

> **7 August 2026 (GHOST CARD INSIDE THE LEGAL BAND — client v2.6.57 / server v2.6.27, client-only, no restart):** Johan's report with Tim Karlsson's tournament export: the description line "9 times out of 10 you want to pay the upkeep…" parsed as a 9x ghost library card (a 273-char name) and landed the totals at 12/72 — INSIDE the legal band, so both count-based guards passed BECAUSE of the ghost; the receipt's "1 unknown name" and the Deck Lab ⚠ (whose tooltip anticipated exactly this class) were informative only. Two layers, each covering the other's blind spot. (1) parseDeck preamble-prose rule: when BOTH a Crypt and a Library header are present, qty-lines BEFORE the first header are description prose → skipped lines, visible in the receipt. Battery-verified against TWD/VDB/Lackey/headerless BEFORE building: kills Tim's ghost (true 12/63) AND retro-heals the original B-53 ghost ("90 cards of pure aggro" now parses to the TRUE 12/60 instead of being refused as a 12/150); saved decks self-heal since every consumer re-parses the stored text. Lackey (only "Crypt:") and headerless pastes untouched — their pre-header entries ARE the cards. No name-shape heuristic: vtes.json is not on disk to verify one against, and the protective-cap lesson already bit once. (2) The unknown-name gate (Johan's own proposal): `deckUnknownNames` (the receipt's own DB check, now the ONE source — updateImpCount reads the same list) + `deckUnknownText` at impOk AND the offline start — tournament refuses with the escape hatch taught ("turn off Tournament rules … custom or house cards"), casual confirms, DB absent stays quiet (the wave-3 convention). Upgrades the wave-3 answer matrix: misspellings go from "surfaced" to "blocked under tournament rules". Named concessions: the zero-dependency server has no card DB, so tournLock rooms stay size-enforced only (a modified client can smuggle text cards); prose inside a section, or under a single header with the DB off, still reaches play as a text card (the receipt shows it). Gates: client 61→67/0 (destructively verified: exactly the 3 parser-rule tests fail against the pristine client), dispatch 39/0, protocol 6/0, canary 37/0, bot 721/0 alone, B1 byte-identical 1 137 329, check-versions green. > **28 July 2026 (LIVE-FINDINGS ROUND — client v2.6.51 / server v2.6.24, client-only, no restart):** the first two things Johan hit after launching on `elysium.cards`. (1) **Create-time Helpers choice** — Tournament / All helpers on / Player's choice — as a `<select>` in the create dialog, sent on the `create.helperPolicy` field the server has accepted since the policy shipped, so the room is BORN with the right policy; the hotseat dialog gained the same three modes (its third reads "Your local settings", since one screen has one set of Settings). The old `joined`-handler fallback that hardcoded `tournament:true` **could never fire** — every 2.6 server stamps `helperPolicy` onto `joined` — so the LIVE default was in fact `allowLocal:true` with every helper on, which is why the choice looked "lost". (2) **Classic free-board park fix:** `revealed()` clamped every ONLINE pile-drop to `zoneRect('ready')`, which on a Classic table is only the TOP 44.5 % band — proven numerically: a drop at (300,470) landed at (4,4) before, (300,470) after. Both offline park paths already carried the `l4on()` branch; the online one never got it. (3) **Pile-park bounce:** newly parked cards nudge to the nearest free spot (threshold 0.55 so tight face-down packing survives, step 1.02 so they land beside rather than at 45 % overlap); park paths only, because a deliberate drag onto a card is the attach gesture. Client suite **29/0 → 40/0**; bot suite re-run **629/0** after a shared-harness change.

**Evaluation round → arena plan revised → T1 EXECUTED (arena v1.1.4, 24 July).** Johan asked for a full evaluation of the bot work, then GO on "review/update the arena plan and run T1" — adding an explicit T6 requirement: reason objects must carry **hand + board state + resources** so decisions are evaluable in the context of the choices that actually existed. The evaluation re-verified everything live in the snapshot (cardfx 186/0 incl. the suite's own disk-matches-fresh-compile against the uploaded vtes.json; bot-logic 401/0×3 with zero flake; coverage numbers reproduced exactly — Malkavian 73/77, Tremere 72/77, Johan's three decks 100%; arena to double-oust clean) and measured the library's scope: **2,364 entries / 1,518 fx-tagged / 54 curated / 3 handlers out of 4,149 cards** — the three-tier model's strongest evidence yet. Five findings in standing-question spirit: **(1) hunting-ground is per-VAMPIRE** ("A vampire can gain blood from only one hunting ground each turn"; Johan confirmed — Papillon + a clan HG may feed two different vampires the same turn); the bot's global `_huntingGroundUsedThisTurn` is stricter than the rule — conservative-only (missed value, never a misplay), zero practical impact today (each tracked deck holds exactly one HG type, verified), queued as a hygiene item. **(2)** The arena's persona↔deck conflation (decks rotate in step with personas — the smoke run's "novice 2/2" was really "the sewer deck 2/2"). **(3)** The BOT_VERSION one-line history is a growing fragility (the newline-in-comment class already bit once). **(4)** Spec §1's snapshot table is frozen at v0.6.10 against code v0.6.42. **(5)** The grinder deck file is absent from the project + 'grinder' unregistered in ARCHETYPES — the superior-Govern path is rig-reachable only. **Roadmap re-recorded** in `elysium-bot-training-backlog.md` (a dated block superseding 16 July where they differ): Steg 1 five-decks-100% (equipment v1 → Misdirection/Pentex handlers → Revelations LAST via the reveal/grant mechanics, never a bot sense; + the HG per-vampire hygiene fix) → Steg 2 the arena as measurement/data machine (T1 → T2 → T3 duplicate seating → **T6 as the backbone** with Johan's context schema: rv:1 + ctx{hand, board digest, resources} + options considered-AND-gate-excluded-with-reasons + chosen + persona knobs; hand contents are bot-LOCAL telemetry to the trace channel, never table-visible — the fairness boundary governs senses, not self-reporting → T4 → T9b; P1 silent politics at the step's tail) → Steg 3 politics (needs a vote deck — `votes` tagged on 60 cards, zero consumed — plus the grinder file recovered). **Arena v1.1.4:** per-match wall-clock + event counts in results, a games/hour extrapolation in the summary, `--bots` lifted 2–5 (server MAX_PLAYERS=6 source-verified first), and the conflation warning whenever decks differ. Gates with the patch: cardfx 186/0 · canary 37/0 · bot-logic 401/0×3 · client 29/0 · dispatch 19/0 · protocol 6/0 · versions ✓. **T1 numbers (sandbox — Johan's hardware shifts absolutes; magnitudes/sensitivities transfer):** 3 bots/pool 12 (6 matches) = **34.3 s/match · 3,743 ms/player-turn · ≈105 games/hour** · 4 bots ≈73/h · 5 bots ≈62/h (5-seat runs CLEAN: full oust ladders, no hangs) · pool 30 ≈45/h at **5,652 ms/turn** (turns LENGTHEN as boards grow, not just multiply) · ask-secs 2→4 **FLAT** (3,743→3,566) — bot-vs-bot handshakes resolve on ANSWER, not timeout; the thinkMs suspicion was killed in SOURCE without a single run (the react-delay branch collapses to 40 ms at paceMs 0 — the persona knob is structurally inert in arena mode). **L2 arithmetic: a 20-candidate × 20-game generation ≈ 400 games ≈ 4 h — "a night", not "a month."** Strategy §7's first risk resolved + §10 decision-log entry added; the T1 backlog entry carries the full matrix.

**Standing-question round on T1 (arena v1.1.5, same day).** Johan's standing question — four uncertainties declared, two MEASURED on the spot instead of waved: *(A)* the original 4-seat clamp's origin — a grep across the whole bot found ZERO hardcoded seat assumptions (the only "4" in seat context is the rulebook's own `min(4, seat)` opening-transfer stagger, seat-generic and correct) — the clamp judged arbitrary, the 5-seat lift stands; *(I)* deck-independence — wall/sewer/wall (combat-heavy mix) measured **2,590 ms/player-turn** (≈97/h, spread 29.8–47.9 s) vs hacking's 3,743: per-turn cost is DECK-MIX-dependent (±30%) but in the favorable direction (combat-heavy = cheaper, opposite the maneuver-window worry); the "stable unit" claim held across SEATS for a fixed deck and is now scoped via an addendum in the T1 backlog entry. The conflation warning also fired correctly on its first live mixed-deck run — built-in verification for free. **Three fixes (Johan's GO):** the T1 addendum line; a timing-scope comment on `t0` (per-match ms INCLUDES connect+lobby+game, EXCLUDES the finally-teardown ~200ms and the 800ms server boot — games/hour ~1–2% optimistic vs true wall-clock-with-gaps, ms/turn carries a few % setup amortization); and the conflation warning now states its equal-length precondition (pairing constant ONLY when the persona and deck lists are equal length — otherwise it cycles with period lcm(P,D), an imprecision inherited from the v1.1.0 comment's original claim). **Noted without action:** the matches:0 module edge (an Infinity print, CLI-unreachable via the `|| 3` guard) and 6 seats (deliberately unexposed). Gates: arena `--check`/require ✓ · bot-logic 401/0 · versions ✓.

**The R-track is born: Revelations + hand-intel — R0 spike EXECUTED, plan doc delivered (`elysium-bot-revelations-plan.md`, 24 July, same day).** Johan's direction call: Malkavian-100% next (revising steg 1 — Revelations FIRST, equipment/Tremere after), with three new client features as enablers: bots signal via chat, seated players' chat renders as the quick-phrase bubble, and an inf/sup choice at play with mode text in the animation+log. Two design decisions frozen in discussion: the chooser sits AFTER the play gesture but BEFORE push/fx/log with a FREE Cancel (nothing has hit the wire), and the **human-backup constant** — humans must ALWAYS be able to handle the bot's elements manually (the v2.6.35 bot-elements exception elevated to a stated principle). **R0 findings (all source-verified):** *(1)* card text — [aus] "look at" is PRIVATE to the actor, prey-locked, actor picks the discard; [AUS] is PUBLIC ongoing open hand, persistent, burnable by any minion as a (D) action, and carries **no Ⓓ** (undirected — block-window semantics verified at build). *(2)* An official ruling extracted from vtes.json's own embedded KRCG rulings (the search revealed the database carries them): "You do not get to see the card drawn as replacement" [LSJ 20100119] ⇒ post-inferior intel = knowns minus the discarded card PLUS one unknown slot — the decay model's first rulebook-anchored detail. *(3)* **ZERO new wire verbs needed**: `revealHand` (private list to recipients, no-names pub line to third parties), `openHand` grant/revoke (**the granter's client OWNS the grant state; the server only routes** ⇒ the owner re-sends on hand changes — the bot's compliance duty as prey defined), `logTo` (the server comment literally says "used for target-of-revealed-card"). *(4)* The desktop client ALREADY has the full human UI (openHandMenu, grant/revoke rendering, revealHand surfaces) — R1 needs no new reveal surfaces. *(5)* **`sup` is an existing per-card pub field** with a manual setter since v2.5.58 ⇒ feature C = a pre-commit chooser + auto-set the field + a free-STRING mode text in log/fx.verb — zero field-list changes. *(6)* The bot has ZERO handlers for all four verbs ⇒ R2/R3 build both directions. *(7)* Multi-choice measured: "choose one" = 14 cards; anarch = 60 (38 with 2+ discipline refs); the STACKING class ("More than one Discipline can be used") = exactly 8 cards — a genuinely new mode-COMBINATION concept, documented in the plan's §7, built when a deck demands it; the exclusive-alternatives (Line Brawl) shape is already representable per-mode and the generic chooser covers its UI for free. **The plan:** R0 ✅ → R1 client round (generic n-option chooser with Cancel, prey pre-selection in the globe, chat bubbles at BOTH endpoints with a clamp, spectators excluded) → R2 hand-intel foundation (provenance-gated per-seat store with honest decay; two consumers: the bleed-commit ground-truth override of readP + the discard picker on the insight categories) → R3 the card itself (curated modes, BOTH sides incl. bot-as-prey compliance, burn handling, courtesy chat; bot-initiated burn of an enemy Revelations = a v1.5 candidate) → **Malkavian 77/77**. The two-layer channel frozen: free chat = human courtesy, the machine trigger = the play line. Documentation-only round — no code touched; gates unaffected by definition. **Aftermath (same evening) — the R1 chooser UX frozen (Johan):** highlight follows the vampire's actual level — Superior visually primary iff ANY ready own vampire has the discipline at superior, else Inferior; the client binds no acting minion for human plays (sandbox by design), so the check is TABLE-level, the same scan shape as `_modeUsableBy`. SOFT warning when no ready own vampire has the discipline at all — never blocking (allies play cards, discipline grants exist, the check sees printed crypt data only; the table's call stands). The popup is classified a TABLE-HELPER (new key `modeChoice` in HELPER_DEFS + the server HELPER_KEYS whitelist, auto-off in Tournament mode, host-lockable via setHelperPolicy — ONE server line, restart once; the client-only tournLock-gating lightweight alternative documented but not default) while the v2.5.58 manual sup setter stays a plain always-available feature — the human-backup constant in practice. Consequence on record: wire mode text becomes helper-dependent, so bot parsers treat it as OPTIONAL enrichment with inference as the fallback (already the backward-tolerance plan). Plan sections 3.C/8/9 updated.
